  validation. The flawed test assertion was introduced after the original
  4.8.0 baseline by `86e45c90b4`.
- **Root cause:** `feature_loadblock.py` expects `getblock` to report
  `Block not found on disk` for the imported invalid block. Import may write
  and index a candidate block before `ConnectBlock` rejects it, so the RPC
  can still return its bytes while the block remains invalid and inactive.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `test/functional/feature_loadblock.py:121-122`,
  `LoadblockTest.run_test`; `src/rpc/blockchain.cpp:1609-1611`,
  `getchaintips` invalid status.
- **Exploitability:** Test-oracle failure, not demonstrated invalid-block
  acceptance. The node log records `bad-txns-transfer-asset-bad-deserialize`
  and `InvalidChainFound` at height 101 while the active chain remains at
  height 100.
- **Expected behavior:** The test must assert that import retains the valid
  height-100 best chain and reports the height-101 candidate as invalid,
  regardless of whether its raw bytes remain on disk.
- **Proposed remediation:** Query `getchaintips` for the exact candidate hash
  and require `status=invalid`, along with the existing best-tip equality.
- **Regression required:** Re-run `feature_loadblock.py` with Python 3.10 on
  the unchanged binary; before the test correction it fails solely on the
  erroneous `getblock` assertion.
- **Remediation commit:** `a613d7029` replaces the disk-presence oracle with
  an exact-hash `getchaintips` invalid-status assertion.
- **Verification:** Before correction, the Python 3.10 test failed at the
  `getblock` assertion while the node log showed `ConnectBlock` rejection
  and `InvalidChainFound`. After correction, the same test passed using the
  unchanged wallet-enabled binary and retained its active-chain assertions.
  The three independent asset DB crash/missing-block tests also passed.
  Python 3.12 cannot import this legacy P2P framework's `asyncore`; that
  interpreter failure is separate from the test-oracle defect.
- **Gate follow-up:** `fa1c56bbc` includes this test in both required CI
  selections. It passed in the expanded 24-test local run.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA CI remains pending.

### FINDING-130: Bit-8 rollback retains a now-invalid asset transfer in mempool

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A reorg across the transfer-script activation can
  leave a transaction in the live mempool that a restarted node would reject.
  A miner can first select it for an invalid template; block validation still
  rejects it, so invalid-block acceptance is not demonstrated.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `6143bf3f1`, before regression or remediation.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Activation and reorg policy must not depend
  on node process history, including asset transfers near a PQ asset phase.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** The inherited transfer-script-size deployment
  on bit 8 and its dynamic payload parser.
- **Root cause:** `CTxMemPool::removeForReorg` checks finality, coinbase
  maturity, and PQ rollback, but never reparses asset transfer payloads
  against the new best tip's bit-8 state. The preactivation parser uses a
  different offset for `OP_PUSHDATA1` transfers.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/txmempool.cpp:790-845`,
  `CTxMemPool::removeForReorg`; `src/validation.cpp:445-482`,
  `UpdateMempoolForReorg`; `src/assets/assets.cpp:668-700`,
  `TransferAssetFromScript`.
- **Introducing provenance:** Inherited from official Core 4.8.0 merge
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`. Its
  `removeForReorg` at `src/txmempool.cpp:790-825` does not revalidate asset
  transfers after a reorg, whereas fresh admission reparses them. The
  official merge and its 4.8.0 version-bump parent
  `b60f50e04f1fba425b28804e61be2694faaf3469` have identical versions
  of all four affected implementation files. This is not introduced by the
  RIP-25 integration.
- **Exploitability:** Confirm an asset before activation, admit a long-message
  transfer after activation without mining it, then invalidate the activation
  block. The live mempool may retain it while fresh admission under the same
  LOCKED_IN chain rejects it. Block assembly can initially select the stale
  transaction and fail final template validation.
- **Expected behavior:** After a bit-8 rollback, every remaining transfer
  satisfies the new tip's parser rules or is removed with descendants.
- **Remediation:** `cd329466ced53b8d8b9e7873203ea4aaf3a25083` passes the
  new tip's `TxAssetDeploymentContext` into `removeForReorg` and runs the
  same context-dependent structural `CheckTransaction` parser used by
  admission. It removes invalid transactions with descendants and applies
  the existing admission gate for pre-asset-activation outputs. It does not
  alter consensus block validation or the historical preactivation parser.
  It deliberately avoids a full `CheckTxAssets` rescan of every mempool
  transaction, which would add asset database work under both chain and
  mempool locks. It could also evict dependent issue-plus-transfer pairs
  for unrelated reasons.
- **Regression:** `f94903cbe` added a red/green functional vector for an
  unmined `OP_PUSHDATA1` transfer across ACTIVE to LOCKED_IN. It compares
  live mempool contents with fresh admission and keeps a valid short-transfer
  control.
- **Verification:** Before the fix the transfer survived rollback although
  fresh admission rejected it with `bad-txns-transfer-asset-bad-deserialize`.
  After the fix the test passed five consecutive standalone runs and the
  required 25-test functional selection passed with Python 3.10. Wallet and
  no-wallet `make check` passed; both workflows now require the new test.
  Python 3.12 cannot import the legacy framework's `asyncore`, so the local
  functional selection used the same Python 3.10 generation as Ubuntu 22.04.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA CI confirmation pending.

### FINDING-128: Historical replay hides a same-block asset issuance

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A valid block can fail `VerifyDB(4)` and prevent a
  node configured with `-checklevel=4` from starting. The default startup
  level is 3, so this is not demonstrated consensus acceptance divergence.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `4854f46ea`; identified by independent review
  before remediation or regression-test changes.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Historical asset verification must reproduce
  the result of normal block connection on the same chain.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Asset metadata and transaction validation.
  This parent-child replay regression was introduced by integration commit
  `335095437`, not inherited from official Core 4.8.0.
- **Root cause:** `VerifyDB` reconnects each block through a fresh child
  asset cache. Disconnection leaves a new-asset removal tombstone in the
  historical parent. A later transaction in the same replayed block can
  transfer the newly issued asset, but metadata and existence lookups test
  the parent tombstone before the child's new-asset addition.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:5268-5273`,
  `CVerifyDB::VerifyDB`; `src/assets/assets.cpp:3398-3424`,
  `CAssetsCache::CheckIfAssetExists`; `src/assets/assets.cpp:3482-3500`,
  `CAssetsCache::GetAssetMetaDataIfExists`; `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:775-785`,
  `CheckTxAssets`.
- **Exploitability:** A consensus-valid block issues an ordinary asset and
  spends its issuance output into a transfer later in that block. Normal
  connection sees the metadata in its block cache. Level-4 replay can
  return `bad-txns-transfer-asset-not-exist` for the same transaction.
- **Expected behavior:** Local child additions override older parent
  removals; local child removals override both. Replaying the same block
  must match normal connection without altering consensus asset rules.
- **Proposed remediation:** Correct local-versus-parent precedence in both
  asset existence and metadata queries. Preserve the per-block child cache
  that fixed FINDING-124.
- **Regression required:** A red unit case with a historical parent
  tombstone and same-block child issue, plus an end-to-end valid
  issue-and-transfer block followed by `verifychain(4,1)` and restart with
  `-checklevel=4`.
- **Remediation commits:** `f071a3dc5` adds the red unit test;
  `446084c97` makes current-block cache entries outrank historical parent
  tombstones; `af4c01ae6` adds the two-node full-chain replay test; and
  `fa1c56bbc` requires that functional test in both CI gates.
- **Verification:** Before the source fix, the unit test failed both
  `CheckIfAssetExists` and `GetAssetMetaDataIfExists` assertions. After the
  fix, it passed all five assertions. A fresh verifier node accepted a
  valid block with an issue followed by its dependent transfer, then passed
  `verifychain(4,1)` and restarted with `-checklevel=4`. Nine existing
  historical/reorg functional regressions, wallet and no-wallet `make check`,
  and the expanded 24-test required functional selection passed. Independent
  diff review found no additional cache-precedence defect.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA CI remains pending.

### FINDING-129: Required CI gate omits asset recovery and import regressions

- **Severity:** HIGH for release qualification. A green required functional
  gate can omit the tests that reproduce asset-state loss, recovery failure,
  and invalid block import.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `8b4acb90a`, before gate changes.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Asset state and validation must survive
  restart and block import without bypassing consensus rules.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Chainstate-ahead rebuild and asset DB
  durability, including FINDING-113, FINDING-114, and FINDING-116.
- **Root cause:** Both required workflow command lists omit
  `feature_asset_dbcrash.py`, `feature_asset_dbcrash_transfer.py`,
  `feature_asset_db_missing_block.py`, and `feature_loadblock.py` although
  these security regressions exist and are registered in the test runner.
  The new FINDING-128 same-block replay regression must also be registered
  and required before final qualification.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `.github/workflows/build-raven.yml:82-90`
  and `.github/workflows/rip25-v48-final-gate.yml:104-112`, required
  functional commands; `test/functional/test_runner.py:68-80,192`, test
  registration.
- **Exploitability:** A later source regression could restore asset loss or
  invalid imported-block acceptance while mandatory CI remains green.
- **Expected behavior:** Both required gates execute all five named
  regressions and fail on any test failure or skip.
- **Proposed remediation:** Add the existing four tests and the new replay
  test to both workflow lists. Ensure the new test is executable and listed
  by the functional runner, then run the exact required selection locally.
- **Regression required:** Validate test list parsing, run the expanded
  `--require-tests` selection, and confirm a missing test exits nonzero.
- **Remediation commit:** `fa1c56bbc` updates both required workflow lists
  and registers the new executable replay test.
- **Verification:** Both workflow YAML files parse. The exact expanded local
  selection passed 24 of 24 with `--require-tests` under Python 3.10; an
  unknown required test exited nonzero with an explicit missing-test error.
  The standalone gate unit script was not qualified from this out-of-tree
  build because it hard-codes the absent in-source `test/config.ini`; CI
  uses an in-source configure. Exact-SHA CI is still required.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA CI remains pending.

### FINDING-131: Raw PQ signing self-check omits contextual witness-v2 flag

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. Valid native PQ signatures are produced but the RPC
  incorrectly reports an incomplete transaction, obstructing manual anchor
  construction and asset migration. No invalid-block acceptance is shown.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at the bit 13 candidate before the RPC correction;
  the base HEAD was `1416e69ea20e636cef46c663bba421a912c9f0e1`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** An activated witness-v2 spend must verify
  under the same contextual PQ script flag used at admission and block
  connection.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The inherited raw-signing self-check
  used standard policy flags; the defect becomes observable only after
  integrating RIP-25 witness-v2 signing.
- **Root cause:** `signrawtransaction` signed the native PQ input, then used
  `STANDARD_SCRIPT_VERIFY_FLAGS` alone for `VerifyScript`, treating version 2
  as reserved. Its own result reported `complete=false` and a reserved
  witness-version error for a valid signature.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/rpc/rawtransaction.cpp:2040-2045,
  2073-2080`, `signrawtransaction`.
- **Provenance:** The base standard-flags call predates Core 4.8.0; the
  network-context signature integration in `4f5c86866` did not add the
  contextual PQ flag at this self-check. This is a RIP-25 integration gap,
  not an official 4.8.0 security regression.
- **Exploitability:** A user constructing a protected asset transfer with a
  native PQ anchor receives a false signing failure and may abandon or work
  around migration. Consensus verification still rejects bad signatures.
- **Expected behavior and remediation:** Add `SCRIPT_VERIFY_PQ_HYBRID` to the
  self-check only when native RIP-25 is active for the current chain tip;
  preserve the fixed PQ sighash and the existing signature checker context.
- **Regression:** `feature_pq_asset_anchor.py` signs a real native ML-DSA
  anchor in a tagged asset transfer. The first run failed with the reserved
  witness-version error; after the correction it reports `complete=true`,
  admits the valid transaction, and rejects a corrupted PQ signature.
- **Remediation commit:** `36b016b6d`; full-chain regression in
  `26aaec534`.
- **Current status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA CI and release qualification
  pending.

### FINDING-132: Boundary mempool clear leaves stale asset and compact-block state

- **Severity:** HIGH. A pending asset reissue can remain locked after its
  transaction disappears, and stale compact-block iterators can be
  dereferenced after the new activation boundary.
- **Initial status:** OPEN in the uncommitted bit 13 candidate based on
  `1416e69ea20e636cef46c663bba421a912c9f0e1`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** A bit 13 activation transition must cleanly
  evict invalid old-policy transactions without retaining node-local
  mempool state that changes admission or destabilizes P2P processing.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** The unsafe incompleteness of the inherited
  `CTxMemPool::_clear` side-table cleanup predates this integration. The
  newly introduced live activation call exposed it; this candidate did not
  create the old `_clear` implementation.
- **Root cause:** The first bit 13 candidate called `mempool.clear()` at the
  boundary. `_clear` erases `mapTx` without clearing `vTxHashes` or the
  persisted global `mapReissuedAssets` / `mapReissuedTx` reservations.
  Ordinary staged per-transaction removal performs their cleanup.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:3619-3637`,
  `ConnectTip` activation handling; `src/txmempool.cpp:1112-1150`, `_clear`;
  `src/txmempool.cpp:645-650`, per-transaction reissue cleanup;
  `src/blockencodings.cpp:113-120`, compact-block iterator use;
  `src/assets/assetdb.cpp:101-114`, reissue-state persistence.
- **Exploitability:** Leave a legacy reissue in the mempool just before bit
  13 becomes effective. The activation clear removes its transaction but
  retains the reissue reservation, so a different valid reissue is rejected
  with `bad-tx-reissue-chaining-not-allowed`; persisted state can survive a
  restart. A matching compact-block short ID can dereference an iterator
  into the erased transaction map, creating a P2P-reachable crash risk.
- **Expected behavior and remediation:** After connecting the boundary tip,
  revalidate the mempool against that exact next-block context and remove
  invalid roots plus descendants through `RemoveStaged`, preserving all
  ordinary index and reservation cleanup. Never invoke the inherited broad
  `_clear` on a live, populated mempool for this transition.
- **Regression:** `feature_pq_asset_anchor.py` submits a pending reissue
  before the boundary, crosses it, rolls back, and submits a distinct
  re-signed reissue. Before the fix the second send failed with
  `bad-tx-reissue-chaining-not-allowed`; after staged removal it succeeds.
  The test also covers subsequent boundary reconsideration and asset
  transaction eviction. Compact-block iterator safety follows from using
  the existing staged removal path and still needs sanitizer/P2P testing.
- **Remediation commit:** `cdca51037`; red/green functional regression in
  `26aaec534`.
- **Current status:** FIXED locally for the new activation path.
  The inherited `_clear` defect is separately remediated under FINDING-133;
  exact-SHA CI and release qualification remain pending.

### FINDING-133: Core 4.8.0 live mempool clear retains dangling indexes and reissue locks

- **Severity:** HIGH with a live-clear precondition. The compact-block index
  retains iterators into erased transactions, and reissue admission can remain
  blocked after `clearmempool` or miner autofix empties the pool.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `1416e69ea20e636cef46c663bba421a912c9f0e1`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Live mempool mutations must not turn later
  P2P compact-block processing into invalid iterator access, and must not
  create process-history-dependent asset admission.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** This is a separate bug already present in
  official Core 4.8.0, not a missing named 4.8.0 security backport.
- **Root cause:** `CTxMemPool::_clear` erased `mapTx` but left `vTxHashes`
  pointing into it, as well as address/spent indexes and global pending
  reissue reservations. `clearmempool` invokes this method on a live node;
  `-autofixmempool` can invoke it after a failed mining template.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/txmempool.cpp:1112-1150`, `_clear`;
  `src/rpc/blockchain.cpp:1881-1896`, `clearmempool`;
  `src/miner.cpp:217-226`, optional autofix path;
  `src/blockencodings.cpp:113-120`, compact-block iterator dereference;
  `src/assets/assetdb.cpp:101-114`, reissue-state persistence.
- **Introducing provenance:** Official Core 4.8.0 commit
  `b60f50e04f1fba425b28804e61be2694faaf3469` has the same incomplete
  `_clear` body. The issue was not introduced by RIP-25 integration.
- **Exploitability:** After an authorized live clear with a populated pool,
  a peer's compact block can match a stale short ID and dereference a dead
  `txiter`. A different reissue of the same asset is rejected with
  `bad-tx-reissue-chaining-not-allowed` although the old transaction is absent.
  The P2P crash path is plausible from control flow; sanitizer reproduction
  remains a separate qualification step.
- **Expected behavior and remediation:** Before erasing `mapTx`, release
  per-entry reissue reservations and estimator state, and clear compact-block
  and address/spent indexes. Preserve unrelated fee-prioritization behavior.
- **Regression:** `mempool_clear_drops_compact_block_index` fails before the
  fix because `vTxHashes` is nonempty after `clear()`, and passes afterward.
  `feature_mempool_clear_reissue.py` fails before the fix with
  `bad-tx-reissue-chaining-not-allowed` after `clearmempool`, and passes
  afterward by admitting and mining a distinct re-signed reissue.
- **Remediation commit:** `8e19cf8d1`.
- **Current status:** FIXED locally. Both red/green regressions passed, as
  did the 29-test mandatory functional selection and clean `make check`;
  exact-SHA CI and sanitizer review remain pending.

### FINDING-134: Core 4.8.0 restores reissue reservations without their mempool transactions

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A restart can block a legitimate reissue until the
  stale reservation is removed, even though no conflicting transaction is
  present. An inflation or invalid-block acceptance path is not shown.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `1416e69ea20e636cef46c663bba421a912c9f0e1`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Asset admission after restart must derive
  from the active chain and actual mempool, not a separately persisted
  reservation snapshot.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** This is an inherited Core 4.8.0 asset-mempool
  recovery bug, not introduced by the RIP-25 integration.
- **Root cause:** Startup loaded `mapReissuedAssets` from the asset database
  before loading `mempool.dat`. With `-persistmempool=0`, the map could name a
  transaction that would never be restored. It could also disagree with a
  missing or stale mempool file.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/init.cpp:1609-1623`, asset database
  startup; `src/assets/assetdb.cpp:101-115`, persisted reissue map;
  `src/validation.cpp:5964-6025`, transaction-by-transaction mempool load;
  `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:870-872`, anti-chaining check.
- **Introducing provenance:** The separate reissue-map persistence and load
  are present in official Core 4.8.0 commit
  `b60f50e04f1fba425b28804e61be2694faaf3469`.
- **Exploitability:** Create an unconfirmed reissue, stop with
  `-persistmempool=0`, and restart with wallet rebroadcast disabled. The
  mempool is empty but a different valid reissue of the same asset fails
  `bad-tx-reissue-chaining-not-allowed`.
- **Expected behavior and remediation:** Do not restore the independent
  reservation snapshot at startup. Let accepted mempool transactions,
  including transactions loaded from `mempool.dat`, rebuild reservations.
  Existing stored asset metadata and chainstate remain untouched.
- **Regression:** `feature_mempool_reissue_restart.py` reproduced the stale
  rejection before the fix. Afterward, a distinct reissue is accepted with
  mempool persistence disabled. A positive control with persistence enabled
  confirms that the restored reissue is present, has a nonempty reservation,
  and that `clearmempool` releases both before a replacement is admitted.
- **Remediation commit:** `4013287ca`.
- **Current status:** FIXED locally. The red/green restart regression and
  its persisted-mempool positive control passed; exact-SHA qualification
  pending. The old database key is ignored rather than migrated or erased.

### FINDING-135: Suspected executable bit 13 PQ tail, disproved

- **Initial classification:** HIGH at `fdaa3d093`. This was a deliberately
  frozen adversarial hypothesis, not a confirmed vulnerability.
- **Suspected RIP-25 invariant:** The dependent asset rule must preserve the
  classical signature result while validating a matching ML-DSA-44 anchor.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None.
- **Initial theory:** The candidate replaces an asset script's final
  `OP_DROP` with 32 raw program bytes. If the script interpreter executed
  those bytes, a `6d1e` prefix would act as `OP_2DROP PUSH30` and could
  replace a failed P2PKH signature result with a true stack item. A `6a`
  prefix would act as `OP_RETURN` and make the output unspendable.
- **Relevant file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assets.cpp:3631-3705`,
  `GetPQAssetProgram`; `src/script/script.h:579-585`, `CScript::GetOp2`;
  `src/script/interpreter.cpp:1120-1121`, `EvalScript`.
- **Disproof:** `CScript::GetOp2` advances the program counter to the end
  immediately upon `OP_RVN_ASSET`; every following byte is data, not an
  opcode. A focused `VerifyScript` test accepts the structural `6d1e` tag
  but rejects an empty classical signature with `SCRIPT_ERR_EVAL_FALSE`.
  A valid classical signature succeeds with that tag and with tags beginning
  `6a` or `ff`. No bypass or tag-induced unspendability was reproduced.
- **Legacy parser check:** The tail is exactly 32 bytes. Optional transfer
  and reissue hash parsing begins only with at least 33 remaining bytes.
  The canonical parser requires an explicit expiry field when a transfer
  message exists, leaving exactly 32 tag bytes for legacy deserialization.
  Thus the suspected metadata reinterpretation was also not demonstrated.
- **Regression:** `pq_asset_tail_cannot_bypass_classical_signature_test`
  passes 12 focused assertions without changing consensus code.
- **Final status:** FALSE POSITIVE. No production remediation is justified
  by this hypothesis. Independent asset-consensus review remains part of
  FINDING-067 release qualification.

### FINDING-136: Wallet classifies bit 13 asset ownership from the classical key alone

- **Severity:** HIGH for an asset-inclusive wallet release. A tagged owner
  token can be displayed as spendable without the required PQ key or anchor.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `fdaa3d093`; default public-network bit 13
  parameters are dormant.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Hybrid asset custody must reflect both the
  classical spending condition and the dependent PQ authorization.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. Core 4.8.0 has no bit 13 asset rule.
- **Root cause:** `IsMine` recognizes an asset's P2PKH destination only;
  wallet asset coin listing trusts that result. The wallet neither matches
  the tagged program to its PQ key store nor selects a matching native
  witness-v2 anchor for a spend.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/script/ismine.cpp:159-204`,
  `IsMine`; `src/wallet/wallet.cpp:2944-3001,4028-4046,4215-4235`,
  asset listing, coin selection, and signing; `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:648-679`,
  consensus anchor requirement.
- **Introducing commit:** The classical-only ownership logic is inherited,
  but this mismatch is introduced by the new rule in `cdca51037`. It is not
  a defect in official Core 4.8.0 under its original rules.
- **Exploitability:** A wallet with the classical key but not the matching
  PQ key can report a tagged administrative token as available and construct
  an asset transaction which bit 13 rejects. A user can mistake the display
  for recoverable custody.
- **Expected behavior and remediation:** Define and persist the composite
  asset destination, require both keys for spendable classification, select
  and replenish a matching funded PQ anchor, and test encrypted backup and
  recovery. Preserve pre-activation legacy ownership classification.
- **Regression required:** Wallet and functional vectors for normal, owner,
  reissuable, unique, restricted, and qualifier assets, missing PQ key,
  missing anchor, change, migration, and encrypted recovery.
- **Current status:** MITIGATED, still OPEN for release. The height-aware
  filters in commit `8fb90c0aa` now exclude a post-activation asset unless
  the wallet has its tagged PQ key. The
  two-node `feature_pq_asset_wallet_ownership.py` regression reproduces
  false normal-asset and owner-token balances before the filter, verifies
  their absence afterward, and proves that a pre-activation tag-shaped
  output remains classically owned. `feature_pq_asset_anchor.py` also
  checks that a wallet with both keys still reports the protected asset.
  This does not provide an anchor-capable asset transaction builder, a
  composite receiver address, or complete height-aware ownership through
  every transaction reporting and debit path. Those remain required by
  FINDING-067 before an asset-inclusive release.

### FINDING-137: Invalid locked asset outpoint is dereferenced before its bound check

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. Authenticated local RPC input can cause an
  out-of-bounds read and potentially crash the asset-control GUI. No remote
  P2P trigger or consensus effect has been demonstrated.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `eb3469066`; inherited from official Ravencoin
  Core 4.8.0 tag `v4.8.0` (`22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`).
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** None. The bug predates RIP-25 asset support.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None of the listed 4.8.0 consensus fixes;
  this is a wallet-memory-safety defect already present in that release.
- **Root cause:** `lockunspent` validates a nonnegative output index but not
  that the wallet transaction contains it. `LockCoin` stores the outpoint.
  `ListAssets` reads `vout[output.n].scriptPubKey` before its existing
  `output.n < vout.size()` check.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/wallet/rpcwallet.cpp:2724-2737`,
  `lockunspent`; `src/wallet/wallet.cpp:5146-5149`, `LockCoin`; committed
  `src/wallet/wallet.cpp:3083-3092`, `ListAssets`. The official 4.8.0
  dereference precedes its bound check at `src/wallet/wallet.cpp:2542-2545`.
- **Exploitability:** An authenticated caller locks a wallet transaction ID
  with an out-of-range `vout`, then the asset-control GUI refreshes and
  calls `ListAssets`. The invalid index reaches the former unchecked read.
- **Expected behavior and remediation:** Reject or skip an invalid locked
  outpoint before any access to `vout[output.n]`; preserve ordinary locked
  asset listing.
- **Regression required:** `wallet_tests/ListAssets_ignores_invalid_locked_vout`
  with first-invalid and maximum `uint32_t` indices.
- **Current status:** FIXED by `8fb90c0aa`. The guard now precedes the
  dereference. The focused Boost regression, `make check`, and all 30
  required local functional security tests passed.

### FINDING-138: Parallel functional tests can reuse a released ephemeral port

- **Severity:** MEDIUM for release qualification reliability. This creates
  false CI failures and prevents the required cross-platform build matrix
  from starting. It is not a production-network or consensus defect.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `c07f23520`. The CI gate for that exact SHA,
  run `37244647395`, failed while starting `feature_transfer_sidebranch.py`:
  another process already held its assigned P2P port. The other 29 required
  functional tests passed.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Required security tests must execute
  reliably before release qualification.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The port allocator is inherited from
  official Core 4.8.0 tag `v4.8.0`
  (`22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`).
- **Root cause:** `find_free_port` binds an ephemeral socket, closes it, and
  later starts a node on that number. Parallel test processes can choose
  the same now-free port before either node binds. `PortSeed.n`, already
  unique within the functional runner, was not used.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `test/functional/test_framework/util.py:332-390`, `find_free_port`,
  `p2p_port`, `rpc_port`; `test/functional/test_runner.py:569-581`,
  `TestHandler.get_next`.
- **Exploitability:** A scheduling race in the CI environment. It cannot
  make a required test pass incorrectly, but a false red run blocks the
  build matrix and exact-SHA qualification.
- **Expected behavior and remediation:** Allocate disjoint, deterministic
  P2P and RPC port blocks from the per-test seed, away from the usual OS
  ephemeral range. Keep a bounded node index and never probe then release
  a port.
- **Regression required:**
  `RequiredFunctionalGateTests.test_parallel_test_port_blocks_are_disjoint`
  for 30 simultaneous test seeds, eight node positions, and both port
  classes. Rerun the complete required functional list in parallel and the
  GitHub exact-SHA gate.
- **Current status:** FIXED locally by `860be4bc6`. The new focused test
  and all 30 required local functional tests passed; GitHub qualification
  of the corrected SHA remains pending.

### FINDING-139: Asset fee retry loses selected asset change totals

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. An asset transfer that needs a fee retry without
  input reselection can omit asset change and fail consensus validation.
  Inflation or successful asset destruction has not been demonstrated.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `3245d87726e16d478e60da481313ca3eeedd8173`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** A PQ asset builder must preserve the full
  value of selected asset inputs across every fee-estimation iteration.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None of the listed 4.8.0 consensus fixes.
  This wallet defect is inherited from official Core 4.8.0 tag `v4.8.0`
  (`22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`).
- **Root cause:** `mapAssetsIn` is newly constructed inside the fee loop,
  but it is populated only when `pick_new_inputs` is true. Both the
  no-change fee optimization and the subtract-fee path can set that flag
  false and continue. The next iteration reuses `setAssets` while building
  asset change from an empty map.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/wallet/wallet.cpp:4000-4002,4058-4093,
  4294-4301,4335-4343`, `CWallet::CreateTransactionAll`; the same
  control flow appears in official 4.8.0 `src/wallet/wallet.cpp:3401,
  3458-3492,3693-3701,3734-3742`.
- **Exploitability:** A partial asset transfer, for example one unit from
  a selected 100-unit asset UTXO, can lose its 99-unit change output on a
  fee retry that reuses inputs. The transaction is rejected rather than
  relayed because asset input and output totals do not balance.
- **Expected behavior and proposed remediation:** Retain selected asset
  input totals for the lifetime of the selected input set. Clear and
  repopulate them only when asset inputs are reselected. Keep asset change
  construction identical on every fee iteration.
- **Regression required:** Force a partial asset transfer through the
  subtract-fee or no-change retry path; assert the selected asset total
  appears in recipient plus change outputs and the completed transaction
  passes normal validation. The test must fail with the frozen code.
- **Remediation commit:** `acb8010e3` moves `mapAssetsIn` outside the fee
  retry loop while retaining its existing clear and repopulation on input
  reselection. No consensus rule or asset amount is changed.
- **Regression evidence:**
  `wallet_tests/asset_change_survives_fee_subtraction_retry` failed against
  the frozen code with three units of expected asset change and zero actual
  change. The same test passes after the fix. The full 12-case `wallet_tests`
  suite and `make check` pass locally. The test uses a fee-subtracted RVN
  recipient and a partial five-unit asset input, transferring two units.
- **Current status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA GitHub qualification pending.

### FINDING-140: Missing-block recovery test observes a transient startup tip

- **Severity:** MEDIUM for release qualification. A timing-dependent false
  CI failure skips the required cross-platform build matrix. No production
  consensus or database defect has yet been demonstrated by this run.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `8f2e77d4a7b6aebf4bb401270a2663be64a30144`.
  The exact published SHA `82fd13ce0e99207e157633bb114f5b42279db489`
  failed GitHub run `37264272865`: 29 of 30 required functional tests
  passed, while this test observed a tip different from `asset_block`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Recovery regression tests must distinguish
  persistent chainstate corruption from asynchronous startup progress.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** The test exercises the chainstate-ahead and
  asset DB rebuild protections. The faulty immediate assertion was
  introduced by remediation test commit `ecd4ca077`; it is not inherited
  from official Core 4.8.0.
- **Root cause:** The functional framework returns from node startup when
  RPC responds, before background block import necessarily connects the
  final chain tip. The test asserts the exact tip immediately after start.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `test/functional/feature_asset_db_missing_block.py:56-59`,
  `AssetMissingBlockRecoveryTest.run_test`;
  `test/functional/test_framework/test_node.py:94-107`, startup RPC wait.
- **Exploitability:** A slower runner can reach the assertion while the
  recovered chain is still loading. A persistently wrong tip or asset state
  must remain a failing result.
- **Expected behavior and proposed remediation:** Wait within a fixed
  timeout for the exact expected tip, then retain the existing exact tip
  and asset-data assertions. Do not relax the expected block hash or skip
  the missing-block failure-path test.
- **Regression required:** Preserve the GitHub failure as red evidence;
  rerun this test locally and in the exact-SHA GitHub gate. Confirm a
  permanently wrong tip still times out and fails.
- **Remediation commit:** `8d13f146e` waits at most 60 seconds for the
  exact expected block hash, then retains the exact hash and asset-data
  assertions. A permanently wrong tip still reaches the framework's
  timeout failure path.
- **Verification:** The focused missing-block recovery test passed locally;
  both recovery tests passed together; the entire 30-case required
  functional selection passed with four parallel workers. The original
  exact-SHA GitHub failure remains red evidence, and a corrected-SHA CI
  rerun is required.
- **Current status:** FIXED locally; GitHub exact-SHA qualification pending.

### FINDING-141: Asset replay recovery test observes a transient startup tip

- **Severity:** MEDIUM for release qualification. The false CI failure
  prevents the required cross-platform matrix from running. No production
  chainstate defect is established by this failure alone.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `380bb7f025bcefff2f6dae75c809199c8e4b27b0`.
  GitHub Final Gate run `37304785018` at published SHA `4dc4075a1`
  passed unit and fuzz checks and 29 of 30 required functional tests;
  `feature_asset_dbcrash.py` read a different tip immediately after
  restarting the node.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Recovery tests must certify the final
  asset and coins state rather than an intermediate startup tip.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** The test covers asset DB recovery and
  chainstate consistency. Its immediate-tip assertions were added by
  remediation commit `ecd4ca077`, not inherited from official 4.8.0.
- **Root cause:** RPC availability can precede background import reaching
  the recovered chain tip. Four recovery checkpoints assert the expected
  block hash without first waiting for that state.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `test/functional/feature_asset_dbcrash.py:48-50,62-64,83-85,107-109`,
  `AssetReplayCrashTest.run_test`;
  `test/functional/test_framework/test_node.py:94-107`, startup RPC wait.
- **Exploitability:** A slower CI runner can observe an intermediate tip;
  persistent wrong-tip or asset-data recovery must still fail.
- **Expected behavior and proposed remediation:** Await the exact expected
  tip for at most 60 seconds at each restart, then retain all existing
  exact hash, asset-data, crash-marker, and replay-log assertions.
- **Regression required:** Preserve the GitHub failure as red evidence,
  rerun the focused test and the complete required functional gate,
  including an exact-SHA GitHub rerun. A permanent wrong tip must time out.
- **Remediation commit:** `349e02266` waits at each of the four recovery
  restarts for the exact expected tip, bounded by 60 seconds. All existing
  hash, asset-data, crash-marker, and replay-log assertions remain.
- **Verification:** The focused test passed. Four recovery tests passed in
  three parallel loops, and the complete 30-case required functional gate
  passed locally. A directly exercised permanently false predicate raised
  the framework timeout assertion. Exact-SHA GitHub rerun remains required.
- **Current status:** FIXED locally; GitHub qualification pending.

## Findings frozen for the integrated RIP-25 asset phase

### FINDING-090: Asset preflight checks the wrong wallet in multiwallet mode

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. This causes incorrect asset ownership decisions and
  transaction failures in multiwallet use; an unauthorized spend has not been
  demonstrated.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at the 4.8.1 asset-phase starting commit
  `16d8ed40304377e51ff724797c5fb58e3e8ab23c`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Asset owner-token migration and PQ asset
  spending must select and sign from the wallet requested by the RPC.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The defect predates and is present in the
  official Core 4.8.0 asset wallet path.
- **Root cause:** `VerifyWalletHasAsset` reads `vpwallets[0]` rather than the
  wallet selected by the calling RPC.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assets.cpp:4462-4477`,
  `VerifyWalletHasAsset`; selected-wallet RPC call sites in
  `src/rpc/assets.cpp:1146-1570`.
- **Introducing commit/provenance:** `9843103a0a` from 2018, per local blame;
  inherited in Core 4.8.0, not introduced by RIP-25.
- **Concrete exploitability:** With wallet A first and wallet B selected,
  preflight can reject B's valid asset holdings because A has none, or
  approve based on A and fail later when B's coins are selected. Owner-token
  migration and reissue RPCs can be blocked or misleading.
- **Expected behavior:** Verify holdings against the selected wallet and use
  the same wallet for subsequent asset coin selection and signing.
- **Proposed remediation:** Pass the selected wallet explicitly to the
  preflight function and remove dependence on `vpwallets[0]` there.
- **Regression required:** A two-wallet test in both wallet orders proves
  that the selected wallet's asset holdings alone control preflight, while
  the non-selected wallet cannot satisfy it.
- **Remediation commit:** `c451df48176f895b78e67f5c8f915863b9c32075`.
- **Verification:** The selected-wallet preflight passed in both wallet load
  orders in `wallet_asset_multiwallet.py`. The new out-of-tree daemon binary
  was selected explicitly, avoiding a stale in-tree executable. The asset
  unit suites (36 cases), `rpc_assettransfer.py`, `feature_assets.py`,
  `feature_restricted_assets.py`, and `make check` passed.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; GitHub CI remains pending for the 4.8.1
  asset branch.

### FINDING-091: Asset balance APIs disclose the first wallet's holdings

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. Wallet-scoped RPC and GUI views can show another
  loaded wallet's asset holdings, and reward payout preflight can use the
  wrong wallet's balance.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at the 4.8.1 asset-phase starting commit
  `16d8ed40304377e51ff724797c5fb58e3e8ab23c`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** PQ asset ownership, balances, migration,
  and reward distribution must be attributed to the selected wallet.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The global-first-wallet balance helper
  is inherited from official Core 4.8.0.
- **Root cause:** `GetAllMyAssetBalances` and `GetMyAssetBalance` call
  `vpwallets[0]->AvailableAssets` even when the caller selected a different
  wallet. The wallet-scoped `listmyassets` RPC does not pass its wallet to
  these helpers.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assets.cpp:3831-3872`,
  `GetAllMyAssetBalances` and `GetMyAssetBalance`;
  `src/rpc/assets.cpp:962-995`, `listmyassets`;
  `src/assets/rewards.cpp:342`, payout balance preflight;
  `src/qt/assettablemodel.cpp:48`, asset model refresh.
- **Introducing commit/provenance:** `GetAllMyAssetBalances` uses the first
  wallet since `af970ec4c2` (2019); `GetMyAssetBalance` does so since
  `c5bbb0a9ba` (2019). Both paths are inherited by Core 4.8.0.
- **Concrete exploitability:** With wallet A loaded first and wallet B
  selected, `listmyassets` on B can return A's asset balances, while B's
  assets disappear. A reward payout funded from B can be rejected or
  preflighted against A's balance, although the actual transaction later
  selects B's coins.
- **Expected behavior:** Every wallet-scoped balance query and payout check
  reads the explicitly selected wallet; GUI models use their own WalletModel.
- **Proposed remediation:** Require a non-null `CWallet*` in the balance
  helpers and pass the owning wallet at every call site.
- **Regression required:** A two-wallet test verifies each RPC view reports
  only its own holdings in both load orders and that asset reward balance
  preflight uses the payout wallet.
- **Remediation commit:** `c451df48176f895b78e67f5c8f915863b9c32075`.
- **Verification:** `wallet_asset_multiwallet.py` failed before the balance
  fix because wallet B's `listmyassets` returned wallet A's empty set. It
  passed after the fix, including reversed load order and a restart.
  The same asset unit suites, three existing asset functional tests, and
  `make check` passed. The Qt caller was updated but was not compiled in
  this headless local build; the cross-target CI build is pending.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; a reward-specific regression, Qt build,
  and GitHub CI remain pending for the 4.8.1 asset branch.

### FINDING-099: Reward retries may spend a different loaded wallet

- **Severity:** HIGH. An automatic retry can commit a payout from a wallet
  other than the one that authorized the reward request.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `d5ff39d1ad71cfb42dbba9cba48766e17eeefd35`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Asset and RVN reward spending must remain
  scoped to the selected wallet, including retries and PQ asset payouts.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** The defect is inherited from official Core
  4.8.0 tag `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`; it is not an
  integration regression.
- **Root cause:** `distributereward` initiates the request with its selected
  wallet, but the global reward map contains no wallet association and
  `ConnectTip` retries every request using `vpwallets[0]`.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/rpc/rewards.cpp:411-416`,
  `distributereward`; `src/assets/rewards.cpp:29-41,181-241,385-390`,
  `AddDistributeRewardSnapshot`, `DistributeRewardSnapshot`, and
  `CheckRewardDistributions`; `src/validation.cpp:3630-3633`, `ConnectTip`.
- **Introducing provenance:** Present at official Core 4.8.0
  `src/validation.cpp:3384` and the corresponding rewards paths.
- **Concrete exploitability:** Wallet B requests a distribution but cannot
  complete its first payout. On the next connected block, unlocked wallet A
  happens to be first in the wallet list. The retry may construct and commit
  B's payout from A's RVN or asset holdings, or stall if A is locked.
- **Expected behavior:** Retry only through the originating loaded wallet.
  Unknown or ambiguous ownership must fail closed without spending.
- **Proposed remediation:** Bind each in-process reward request hash to the
  selected wallet name, resolve exactly one loaded wallet by that name on
  retry, and skip an unbound or missing owner. Keep the snapshot hash and DB
  serialization unchanged. Existing code does not reload pending requests
  from the DB on restart, so durable retry requires separate migration work.
- **Regression required:** Two-wallet test with B's failed initial payout
  and funded A; mining a block must not debit A. Funding B and mining again
  must pay from B only. Repeat with reversed wallet order and an asset payout
  when practical.
- **Remediation commit:** `1318a4768`.
- **Verification:** The extended `wallet_asset_multiwallet.py` failed on the
  vulnerable binary: wallet A paid 2,000 RVN from wallet B's pending request.
  It passed on the corrected binary: A did not pay, and B paid after funding.
  `feature_rewards.py`, clean wallet-enabled `make check`, and clean no-wallet
  `make check` passed. The new wallet binding is deliberately in-process;
  restart recovery remains open as FINDING-100.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA GitHub qualification pending.

### FINDING-100: Pending reward retries are lost after node restart

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A recorded but unpaid reward can remain stuck after a
  routine restart.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `d5ff39d1ad71cfb42dbba9cba48766e17eeefd35`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Wallet-selected asset and RVN rewards must not
  silently lose their retry path.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Inherited reward behavior in official Core
  4.8.0 tag `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`.
- **Root cause:** `mapRewardSnapshots` is populated only during the RPC call.
  `LoadAllDistributeSnapshot` can read persisted requests but has no startup
  caller. The database duplicate check then rejects a repeated request.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/rewards.cpp:22,29-47,390-421`,
  `AddDistributeRewardSnapshot` and `CheckRewardDistributions`;
  `src/assets/snapshotrequestdb.cpp:202-235`, `LoadAllDistributeSnapshot`.
- **Concrete scenario:** A reward request fails for low funds, the node
  restarts, and the wallet is funded. No automatic retry occurs, while a new
  identical `distributereward` call is rejected as a duplicate.
- **Expected behavior:** Persist a wallet identity with the request, reload
  pending requests safely, and retry only with the same uniquely identified
  loaded wallet. Requests without a trustworthy owner must not spend.
- **Proposed remediation:** Add versioned wallet binding to the reward DB and
  a startup reload path. Define a conservative migration for old unbound
  requests and a wallet-unload/reload policy before enabling retry. Account
  for the crash window between transaction commit and payout-record write,
  and bind ownership snapshots to their chain identity across reorgs.
- **Regression required:** Fail a reward for low funds, restart, fund the
  initiating wallet, and verify a single payout from that wallet. Verify old
  unbound records do not charge another wallet.
- **Final status:** OPEN.

### FINDING-101: Restricted and qualifier wallet state uses wallet zero

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. Multiwallet tag and freeze views can omit the selected
  wallet's events and expose another wallet's address associations.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `d5ff39d1ad71cfb42dbba9cba48766e17eeefd35`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Wallet-scoped asset views must not use a
  different wallet, including future PQ asset ownership views.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Inherited from official Core 4.8.0 tag
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`; consensus restricted-asset
  validation uses separate state and is not shown to diverge here.
- **Root cause:** Restricted events are filtered and signaled through
  `vpwallets[0]`, but the restricted-asset view database is node-global.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assets.cpp:5255-5259`,
  `src/validation.cpp:3022-3034`, `src/rpc/messages.cpp:423,467`,
  `src/qt/myrestrictedassettablemodel.cpp:89-104`.
- **Concrete scenario:** Wallet B receives a qualifier tag while wallet A is
  loaded first. B misses its event; querying wallet B can show A's stored
  address/tag associations from the shared view database.
- **Expected behavior:** Collect relevant events for every loaded wallet,
  notify each owner, and filter wallet-facing views by the selected wallet.
- **Proposed remediation:** Separate node-global indexing from per-wallet
  filtering and backfill historical wallet B state after upgrade.
- **Regression required:** Two wallets in both load orders receive distinct
  tag/freeze events. Each RPC and GUI view shows only its own data before and
  after restart.
- **Final status:** OPEN.

### FINDING-102: Message channel subscription uses wallet zero

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A non-first wallet may miss asset-channel messages
  and shared RPC state may cross wallet boundaries.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `d5ff39d1ad71cfb42dbba9cba48766e17eeefd35`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Wallet-specific asset notification behavior
  must remain correct if assets move to PQ ownership.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Inherited from official Core 4.8.0 tag
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`.
- **Root cause:** Historical scan and transfer hooks inspect only
  `vpwallets[0]`; a one-time global startup flag does not account for wallets
  added later. Message RPCs use a shared channel database.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/messages.cpp:221`,
  `src/coins.cpp:284,310`, `src/init.cpp:2034-2044`,
  `src/rpc/messages.cpp:152`.
- **Concrete scenario:** Wallet B owns a message-channel token but wallet A
  is loaded first. B is not automatically subscribed and can miss messages.
- **Expected behavior:** Scan every loaded wallet, backfill on wallet load,
  and distinguish global channel data from wallet-scoped RPC results.
- **Proposed remediation:** Track subscription scan state per wallet and use
  the selected wallet for automatic subscription and display filtering.
- **Regression required:** Two wallets with different channels in both load
  orders, including wallet addition after startup and restart.
- **Final status:** OPEN.

### FINDING-103: Asset test fails to compile in no-wallet builds

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A required build and test configuration cannot
  complete, so release qualification is incomplete.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `a1cca8037948db68b972804139c097531e5842d0`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Mandatory asset compatibility tests must run
  in wallet-enabled and no-wallet configurations.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. This is a new asset-branch test defect.
- **Root cause:** The test constructs `CAssetOutputEntry` outside
  `ENABLE_WALLET`, but includes the header defining it only when the wallet
  is enabled.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `src/test/assets/asset_tx_tests.cpp:22-24,120,167,200`, asset script tests.
- **Introducing commit:** `a828ffaaa6`.
- **Concrete scenario:** A clean no-wallet `make check` fails to compile
  `test/assets/test_raven-asset_tx_tests.o` with an incomplete type error.
- **Expected behavior:** Consensus asset tests compile and run without wallet
  support, without skipping their assertions.
- **Proposed remediation:** Include the type definition unconditionally while
  retaining wallet-only database headers under the wallet guard.
- **Regression required:** Clean no-wallet build and `make check`, plus the
  wallet-enabled asset test suite.
- **Remediation commit:** `bfb1d5440`.
- **Verification:** A clean no-wallet `make check` failed before this fix with
  three incomplete-type errors in `asset_tx_tests.cpp`. The same configuration
  compiled and passed `make check` after the fix. Wallet-enabled `make check`
  also passed; no asset assertion was skipped or weakened.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA GitHub qualification pending.

### FINDING-104: Transfer-script activation survives a reorg incorrectly

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. Two honest processes on the same best chain can
  parse the same asset transfer differently and disagree on block validity.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `75d349b53fbe3d0bd6ba81ee3c50e20667349071`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Contextual activation and reorg safety for
  PQ asset outputs, especially transfers that use `OP_PUSHDATA1`.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** The transfer-script-size deployment on bit 8
  and its parser gate are inherited from official Core 4.8.0 tag
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`.
- **Root cause:** `AreTransferScriptsSizeDeployed` sets a process-global
  `fTransferScriptIsActive` latch after seeing an ACTIVE tip and never clears
  it after a reorg. `TransferAssetFromScript` chooses a dynamic asset-data
  offset only when that latch is true; otherwise it uses hard-coded offset
  31. The offset differs for `OP_PUSHDATA1` asset scripts.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:6074-6083`,
  `AreTransferScriptsSizeDeployed`; `src/consensus/consensus.h:54`, latch;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:663-690`, `TransferAssetFromScript`;
  `src/script/script.cpp:245-263`, dynamic offset;
  `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:269,716`, asset validation callers;
  `src/validation.cpp:683,2726`, mempool and block entry points.
- **Concrete exploitability:** A node observes bit-8 ACTIVE and then follows
  a reorg to a pre-ACTIVE tip. Its latch remains true. A freshly started node
  at that same tip has a false latch. A transfer script whose payload crosses
  the direct-push boundary can be parsed at offset 32 by the first process
  and at offset 31 by the second. The candidate bit-13 PQ asset deployment
  can activate before bit 8 on regtest unless an explicit dependency is added.
- **Expected behavior:** Every consensus parser decision uses the versionbits
  state of the candidate block's own parent. Mempool policy uses the current
  active-chain tip and updates on reorg. No process-history latch may control
  block validity.
- **Proposed remediation:** Thread contextual bit-8 activation through the
  consensus asset parser, preserving historical pre-activation semantics.
  Require bit 8 as an effective prerequisite of the separate PQ asset
  deployment. Keep non-consensus callers explicitly tip-contextual.
- **Regression required:** `OP_PUSHDATA1` transfer vectors at activation
  minus 2, minus 1, activation, plus 1 and across invalidate/reconsider.
  Compare long-running and restarted nodes on the same best chain; both
  must accept or reject identically. Test both mempool and block connection.
- **Reproduction:** On regtest, `feature_transfer_script_reorg.py` reached
  bit-8 ACTIVE, tested a long transfer, invalidated the first ACTIVE tip,
  and compared the live process with a restart on the identical LOCKED_IN
  chain. The live process returned `missing-inputs`, meaning serialization
  passed; the restarted process returned
  `16: bad-txns-transfer-asset-bad-deserialize` for the same raw transaction.
  The vulnerable binary failed the equality assertion as intended.
- **Local mitigation:** The monotonic latch has been removed. The same
  `feature_transfer_script_reorg.py` vector passes on the corrected binary;
  it failed on the pre-mitigation binary. A clean wallet-enabled `make check`
  also passes. The parser is still tip-contextual in candidate-block and
  disconnect paths, so this is not a complete consensus correction.
- **Mitigation commit:** `cb86a3ca566bfc884313eb747cbae2cd6d224b65`.
- **Final status:** MITIGATED locally, OPEN for release qualification.

### FINDING-105: Asset deployment activation survives a reorg incorrectly

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. A live process accepts an asset issue on a
  pre-activation best chain while a freshly started process on that same
  chain rejects it.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `f7b4b5704`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** A PQ asset extension must never rely on
  process-history activation state for asset issuance or validation.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** The original bit-6 asset deployment and its
  consensus gate are inherited from official Core 4.8.0 tag
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`.
- **Root cause:** `AreAssetsDeployed` sets `fAssetsIsActive` on the first
  ACTIVE tip and never resets it after a reorg. Mempool and block connection
  use this process-local latch rather than the candidate chain context.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:6038-6048`,
  `AreAssetsDeployed`; `src/consensus/consensus.h:52`, latch;
  `src/validation.cpp:671-685,2712-2729`, mempool and block gates;
  `src/rpc/assets.cpp:420`, issue RPC gate.
- **Concrete exploitability:** On regtest, reach bit-6 ACTIVE, call an asset
  RPC to set the latch, and invalidate the first ACTIVE tip. A new, fully
  signed issue transaction funded by an older RVN UTXO passes
  `testmempoolaccept` in the live process. A restart on the identical
  LOCKED_IN chain rejects the exact raw transaction. The same stale gate is
  called from `ConnectBlock`, so block validity is process-history-dependent.
- **Expected behavior:** Activation checks for a block use its own parent;
  mempool and RPC checks use the current active tip, with reorg-safe cleanup.
- **Proposed remediation:** Remove the monotonic latch for consensus use,
  thread contextual bit-6 state through block validation, and revalidate
  mempool asset transactions after crossing the boundary in either direction.
- **Regression required:** `feature_asset_activation_reorg.py` must fail
  against the vulnerable implementation and pass after correction. Add
  block-connection vectors at activation minus 2 through plus 2 and test
  invalidate/reconsider and fresh restart.
- **Reproduction:** The vulnerable binary returned `allowed: 1` in the live
  process and `allowed: 0` after restart for the same signed issue on the
  same LOCKED_IN chain. The equality assertion failed as intended.
- **Local mitigation:** The monotonic latch has been removed. The corrected
  `feature_asset_activation_reorg.py` test builds a signed issue while ACTIVE,
  then tests that identical transaction after a reorg and after restart. It
  passes on the corrected binary and fails on the pre-mitigation binary with
  live `allowed: 1` versus restarted `allowed: 0`. A clean wallet-enabled
  `make check` also passes. Candidate-block and disconnect paths still need
  explicit parent context, so this is not a complete consensus correction.
- **Mitigation commit:** `cb86a3ca566bfc884313eb747cbae2cd6d224b65`.
- **Final status:** MITIGATED locally, OPEN for release qualification.

### FINDING-106: Additional Core 4.8.0 deployment latches survive reorgs

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. The affected predicates reach consensus validation.
- **Initial status:** OPEN in the unchanged helper implementations at
  `f7b4b5704`; identified before any remediation to these three helpers.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Historical block validation and candidate
  PQ asset validation must not depend on process history.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Enforced asset values, coinbase asset
  checks, and restricted/qualifier asset deployment.
- **Root cause:** `AreEnforcedValuesDeployed`,
  `AreCoinbaseCheckAssetsDeployed`, and `IsRip5Active` set process-global true
  flags at LOCKED_IN or ACTIVE and never clear them on reorg. The exact latch
  implementations are present in the official Core 4.8.0 source at
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774:src/validation.cpp:5804-5869`.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:6014-6036,6054-6064`,
  the three helpers; `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:313-322,377-389,590,690-705,830-855,884-892`,
  transaction and asset validation; `src/validation.cpp:2927-2932`, coinbase
  value check; `src/assets/assets.cpp:5391-5445`, restricted-transfer check.
- **Concrete divergence scenario:** After a node observes the deployment
  threshold and then returns to an earlier best chain, its flag stays true.
  A fresh node on that same chain derives false. Reissue output values,
  coinbase asset outputs, and restricted/qualifier transactions are therefore
  evaluated under different rules. `feature_rip5_activation_reorg.py`
  reproduced the restricted-asset policy divergence: `listglobalrestrictions`
  was available on the live node but unavailable after restart on the same
  LOCKED_IN chain. Dedicated transaction/block reproductions for the
  enforced-value and coinbase subcases are still required; static control
  flow establishes the process-history dependency but not every transaction's
  exploitability.
- **Unit reproduction:**
  `rip25_versionbits_tests/asset_deployment_queries_rewind_after_active_tip`
  constructs independent LOCKED_IN, ACTIVE, and STARTED branches for each
  deployment using the real regtest thresholds. Before the final two-helper
  correction, it failed only for `enforced values remained active after
  rewind` and `coinbase assets remained active after rewind`; the three
  previously corrected queries passed. It also asserts the special
  enforced-value LOCKED_IN behavior.
- **Expected behavior:** Each block uses its own predecessor's VersionBits
  state; mempool and P2P policy use the current active-chain state.
- **Proposed remediation:** Remove process-history latches and pass explicit
  candidate-parent context through consensus validation and undo. Preserve
  the unusual LOCKED_IN-or-ACTIVE rule for enforced values.
- **Regression required:** ACTIVE-to-preactive reorg and restart vectors for
  all three deployments, including valid and invalid block connection.
- **Local mitigation:** All three process-history latches are removed in the
  local worktree, while the test-only enforced-value and coinbase overrides
  remain available. The RIP5 functional vector
  `feature_rip5_activation_reorg.py` failed before the change with live
  `True` versus restarted `False`, then passed after the change. All three
  activation-reorg tests passed through `test_runner.py --require-tests`,
  and the enlarged synthetic-chain unit test failed only for the two remaining
  latches before the final patch, then passed after it. Fresh wallet and
  no-wallet builds, both `make check` suites, and the three required
  functional tests passed after the final patch and the FINDING-108 test
  isolation correction. The block-context issue is separately FINDING-107.
- **Mitigation commits:** `1a7a2030a8f6fde4d3e04e6140df75463cd16b0e`
  (RIP5), `8a4cd56cac2af9d124033118c69406d29e692ec4` (boundary test),
  `b912239dfd494bcb594cb0190161140b45fcb15d` (value and coinbase).
- **Final status:** OPEN for release qualification.

### FINDING-107: Context-free block checks consume active-tip asset state

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. Side-branch or historical blocks can be judged
  using deployment rules from an unrelated active tip.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `f7b4b5704`, before any contextual refactor.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Candidate blocks, reorgs, and restart/IBD
  must derive one contextual activation state from the candidate parent.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Bit-6 assets, bit-8 transfer-script size,
  enforced values, coinbase asset checks, and RIP5 restricted assets.
- **Root cause:** `CheckBlock` is called before the candidate parent is looked
  up and caches `block.fChecked`, but it calls `CheckTransaction`, which uses
  active-tip predicates and the transfer parser. `DisconnectBlock` also reads
  tip state while undoing a block whose own predecessor can straddle an
  activation boundary. Removing the monotonic latches alone cannot fix this.
- **Introducing baseline:** The official Core 4.8.0 commit
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774` already calls
  `CheckTransaction` from `CheckBlock` at `src/validation.cpp:4032`, with the
  tip-dependent transfer and enforced-value checks at
  `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:269,313`. RIP-25 integration did not invent
  the block-context error, but its separate PQ asset deployment increases
  the number of activation boundaries affected.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:4168-4173,4225,4241-4243,4620-4623,4674-4683`,
  block checks and admission; `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:269,313`,
  tip-dependent checks; `src/assets/assets.cpp:663-690`, parser;
  `src/validation.cpp:1939,1979,2002,2139,3319-3360`, undo context.
- **Concrete divergence scenario:** A node validates a side-branch candidate
  whose parent is pre-bit-8 while its active tip is post-bit-8, or the reverse.
  A long transfer has different parse offsets. The same candidate can then
  be accepted or marked invalid depending on which branch happens to be
  active when `CheckBlock` runs.
- **Direct block-check reproduction:**
  `rip25_versionbits_tests/checkblock_asset_result_does_not_depend_on_unrelated_tip`
  builds one identical candidate block containing an asset-bearing coinbase,
  then calls `CheckBlock` with the same block under synthetic ACTIVE and
  STARTED active tips for bit 10. The test fails on the vulnerable code:
  `CheckBlock judged the same candidate differently after the active tip
  changed`. It bypasses PoW and merkle checks only to isolate this consensus
  predicate; it does not establish network-level exploitability or successful
  block connection. A mined block-level reproduction remains required.
- **Expected behavior:** Context-free checks never consult deployment state;
  contextual asset checks receive the candidate `pindexPrev`; undo uses the
  disconnected block's own parent.
- **Proposed remediation:** Derive immutable asset-rule context from
  `VersionBitsState(pindexPrev, ...)`, move contextual checks out of
  `CheckBlock`, and thread that context through block connection, asset
  parsing, and undo without altering historical activation predicates.
- **Regression required:** Side-branch blocks on both sides of each boundary,
  activation minus 2 through plus 2, disconnect/reconsider, and fresh
  restart comparisons.
- **Partial remediation commit:** `50ef33f7781db0436825a5968c0b83b5e6223680`.
  `CheckBlock` now performs only context-free transaction checks;
  `ContextualCheckBlock` derives asset predicates from the candidate parent
  and runs the full transaction check on admission, reconnection, and
  `VerifyDB`. The outdated block-version check also uses the candidate
  parent. The existing direct block-check red test and a new explicit
  transfer-payload boundary test pass. Both wallet and no-wallet `make check`
  pass on the combined local implementation. The required three activation
  reorg tests and `feature_assets.py`, `feature_assets_reorg.py`, and
  `feature_raw_restricted_assets.py` all pass in one six-test run. The
  extended raw-asset and restricted-asset functional tests also pass when
  invoked directly with Python 3.10.
- **Additional remediation commit:**
  `a361997550ba9d5db274e5ed17462bd891dc0d92` passes an immutable
  candidate-parent deployment context through block connection, asset
  parsing, UTXO and asset-cache updates, disconnection, and rollforward.
  Three new unit cases cover enforced-value, transfer-parser, and messaging
  boundaries. The three cases, wallet and no-wallet `make check`, the RIP-25
  VersionBits unit suite, and a six-test functional activation and asset
  regression batch passed on this source state. One wallet `make check`
  attempt hit the independently tracked FINDING-112 intermittent recovery
  failure; a repeat `make check` and full direct unit suite passed.
- **Mined-boundary test commit:** `8a2e577f44a5c46fa5b9058d9643e22791a177e7`
  mines a real issue at height 863 and an `OP_PUSHDATA1` transfer at height
  864, then verifies the chain before and after invalidate, restart, and
  reconsider. The test passed on the out-of-tree wallet build.
- **Side-branch test commit:** `d144c9eee` mines a real height-862 sibling
  of a height-861 LOCKED_IN parent while the verifier's active tip is at
  height 864 and bit 8 is ACTIVE. The sibling contains a signed long-name
  `OP_PUSHDATA1` transfer whose inputs exist on both branches and whose
  locktime is zero. `AcceptBlock` rejects it specifically with
  `bad-txns-transfer-asset-bad-deserialize`; 26 of 26 required functional
  tests passed on the corrected binary. The new test is mandatory in both
  workflows. It covers candidate-parent admission, not side-branch
  `ConnectBlock` or historical replay after overtaking the active chain.
- **Side-branch connection test commit:** `eb46048f7` extends the same test
  through a longer competing branch containing a valid post-bit-8 transfer,
  actual connection and reorganization, rollback to LOCKED_IN, restart,
  reconsideration, and `verifychain`. It passed twice in the required
  functional runner and once by direct invocation with the clean build's
  `ravend` selected explicitly. One initial direct invocation accidentally
  selected an older executable on `PATH` and returned `inconclusive`; that
  was a test-command error, not a result from the audited binary.
- **Final status:** MITIGATED, still OPEN for release qualification. Nested
  candidate-parent propagation and side-branch connection after overtaking
  are tested. Broader historical replay and second-audit qualification remain
  due.

### FINDING-108: Synthetic VersionBits test reuses stale cache entries

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. This affects reliability of a required security test,
  not production validation directly.
- **Initial status:** OPEN in the wallet-enabled full suite after the
  FINDING-106 regression test was added.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The Core 4.8.0 bit-11 reorg regression must
  run reliably as part of `make check`.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Transfer-overflow activation testing only;
  the production bit-11 fix is not shown to be defective by this failure.
- **Root cause:** `transfer_overflow_active_tip_policy_is_not_sticky` creates
  short-lived synthetic `CBlockIndex` objects, but the process-global
  `versionbitscache` can contain entries for addresses reused from earlier
  tests. The test neither clears the cache before using those synthetic
  pointers nor removes their cache entries before destroying them.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/test/rip25_versionbits_tests.cpp:161-201`,
  `transfer_overflow_active_tip_policy_is_not_sticky`; `src/validation.cpp:2350`,
  global cache.
- **Introducing commit:** `92ff8206793956c40be8cf028ba2f026788a2eed`
  introduced this test during the earlier RIP-25/Core 4.8.0 remediation.
  It is not inherited from official Core 4.8.0.
- **Reproduction:** The individual test passed, while a wallet-enabled full
  `make check` failed at its assertion that bit 11 returns to STARTED. The
  no-wallet full suite passed in that run, consistent with allocator-dependent
  pointer reuse rather than a stable production rejection.
- **Expected behavior:** Synthetic test chains have an isolated VersionBits
  cache and preserve the same ACTIVE-to-STARTED assertion.
- **Remediation:** Clear the global cache under `cs_main` before installing
  synthetic tips and after restoring the original tip. Apply the same isolation
  to the new five-deployment regression test.
- **Regression required:** Wallet and no-wallet full `make check`, with the
  existing bit-11 assertion unchanged.
- **Verification:** Both full suites passed after cache isolation; the new
  five-deployment boundary test also passed in both variants.
- **Remediation commit:** `8a4cd56cac2af9d124033118c69406d29e692ec4`.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA GitHub qualification pending.

### FINDING-109: Empty per-address asset name reaches undefined string access

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. An untrusted malformed transaction reaches undefined
  behavior during transaction validation; a crash has not yet been reproduced
  on the current build.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `58fbe4c301032f2965335f19738a2a1895a0c4b3`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** None directly. This is an asset-validation
  memory-safety issue that must be resolved before release qualification.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Per-address restricted and qualifier null
  data validation. The official Core 4.8.0 source has the same unchecked
  `.front()` call, so this defect is inherited rather than introduced by
  the RIP-25 integration.
- **Root cause:** `AssetNullDataFromScript` accepts the serialized empty
  `asset_name`; `CheckTransaction` stores it in `mapNullDataTxCount`, then
  calls `entry.first.first.front()` without checking whether the string is
  empty. The analogous global null-data path already checks emptiness.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:206-228,349-351`,
  `CheckTransaction`; `src/assets/assets.cpp:854-876`,
  `AssetNullDataFromScript`; `src/script/script.cpp:331-335`, null-script
  recognition.
- **Concrete exploit scenario:** A peer or RPC caller submits a transaction
  with a zero-value `CNullAssetTxData("", 0)` output and a non-null input.
  Normal mempool validation enters `.front()` on the empty name. Whether it
  crashes depends on the standard-library build and compiler behavior.
- **Expected behavior:** Reject the malformed null-data output with a
  deterministic reason before any empty-string indexing or expensive
  contextual asset processing.
- **Proposed remediation:** Add an explicit empty-name guard at the narrow
  validation point and retain the existing name/ownership checks for
  nonempty records.
- **Regression required:** A real `CheckTransaction` negative vector for an
  empty per-address null-data asset name, plus wallet and no-wallet unit
  gates. Run a sanitizer variant if available.
- **Remediation commit:** `7e034d6ffcd9e9e2a8b2908bf60d6e8c74a80e46`.
- **Verification:** The new test failed before the guard because validation
  returned the generic missing-transfer reason after touching the empty
  string. It passed after the guard. Full wallet and no-wallet `make check`
  passed on the combined local implementation. The guard is at
  `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:210`; sanitizer qualification remains pending.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; sanitizer and exact-SHA CI pending.

### FINDING-110: Block-load exception suppresses transfer-deserialization rejection

- **Severity:** HIGH. The block admission path can store a block even after
  contextual asset validation rejects it. Full block-connection acceptance
  has not been demonstrated, so this is not classified as invalid-block
  consensus acceptance.
- **Initial status:** OPEN at `58fbe4c301032f2965335f19738a2a1895a0c4b3`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Reindex, restart, and live admission must
  apply the same candidate-parent consensus rules.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Transfer-script-size deployment. The
  official Core 4.8.0 `AcceptBlock` has the same `fFromLoad` exception.
- **Root cause:** `AcceptBlock` resets a rejected `CValidationState` to valid
  when loading a block from disk and the rejection reason is
  `bad-txns-transfer-asset-bad-deserialize`. The old active-tip parser error
  likely motivated this workaround, but candidate-parent parsing now makes
  it unsafe to retain without a historical compatibility proof.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:4637-4651`,
  `AcceptBlock`; `src/validation.cpp:5539-5640`, `LoadExternalBlockFile`.
- **Concrete divergence scenario:** A malformed transfer is rejected during
  live block admission but its contextual rejection is suppressed when the
  same block is loaded from a block file. `VerifyDB` level 1 then rejects
  it, and later connection may reject it under a different reason.
- **Expected behavior:** Loading an identical block under an identical
  candidate parent cannot turn a consensus validation failure into success.
- **Proposed remediation:** Remove the special exception with explicit
  candidate-parent parsing in place, then validate historical activation
  boundaries before release. Preserve evidence for any historical block
  that relies on the exception.
- **Regression required:** A block-file reload test with a malformed
  transfer, plus historical activation-boundary replay and restart checks.
- **Remediation commits:** `86e45c90b` adds the red block-file regression;
  `911ff7cb3` removes the exception. The non-consensus tooling fix needed for
  out-of-tree execution is FINDING-111.
- **Verification:** An identical malformed block returned
  `bad-txns-transfer-asset-bad-deserialize` through live `submitblock`, but
  the loader stored its bytes before the fix. The test failed because
  `getblock` unexpectedly returned those bytes. After the fix, the same
  test passed and the imported node kept the exact valid 100-block tip.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; replay of the historical mainnet boundary
  and exact-SHA CI remain pending.

### FINDING-111: Block-file test cannot run from an out-of-tree build

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A required live-versus-import regression cannot run
  against a clean out-of-tree build, weakening the release test gate.
- **Initial status:** OPEN while constructing the FINDING-110 regression.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The exact candidate source must be tested
  without copying or generating executables in the source tree.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The same source-relative hash-helper path
  exists in official Core 4.8.0, so the build-layout defect is inherited.
- **Root cause:** `linearize-data.py` always executes
  `src/test/test_raven_hash` relative to its own source-tree location, while
  the out-of-tree build places that binary under the configured build root.
