- **Affected file/function/lines:** `contrib/linearize/linearize-data.py:52-55`,
  `calc_hash_str`; `test/functional/feature_loadblock.py:75-81`, loader test.
- **Reproduction:** The FINDING-110 red regression attempted to run the
  existing block-file test against the clean out-of-tree build and failed
  before block validation with `FileNotFoundError` for the source-tree helper.
- **Expected behavior:** The test supplies the exact hash helper built from
  the current source to the linearizer without placing binaries in the
  source tree or changing consensus code during tests.
- **Proposed remediation:** Allow the linearizer configuration to specify an
  executable hash-helper path and have the functional test pass the current
  build's helper. Keep its existing source-relative default for other users.
- **Regression required:** The block-file functional test must run from a
  clean out-of-tree build and still test linearization plus invalid-block
  rejection.
- **Remediation commit:** `86e45c90b`.
- **Verification:** The test initially failed with `FileNotFoundError` for
  the source-tree helper. With the configured build helper, linearization
  completed for 101 block hashes and the live-versus-import regression ran
  red before FINDING-110's fix and green after it.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; exact-SHA CI pending.

### FINDING-112: Full wallet unit suite intermittently fails recovery tests

- **Severity:** MEDIUM pending root-cause classification. This is a release
  test-gate reliability problem, not proof of a production wallet-recovery
  failure.
- **Initial status:** OPEN after two failures on the same source state.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Wallet recovery and encryption regressions
  must execute reliably as required release tests.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Unknown. These particular PQ wallet tests
  were introduced during RIP-25 hardening, but the possible underlying
  Berkeley DB issue has not been attributed.
- **Observed failure:** Two successive `make check` runs of the same wallet
  build failed, first at
  `recovery_handles_dump_larger_than_locked_pool_limit`, then at
  `partial_recovery_installs_atomically_and_preserves_backup`. Each named
  recovery test passed alone, the PQ wallet suite passed alone, and a later
  complete `test_raven` run and third `make check` passed without a source
  change between the failures and successes.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `src/wallet/test/pq_wallet_tests.cpp:1485-1503,1540-1551`, recovery tests;
  `src/wallet/db.cpp:285-465`, `CDB::RecoverInternal`. The failing return
  branch has not yet been identified.
- **Expected behavior:** Identical source and fixtures produce consistent
  pass/fail results, with enough failure detail to diagnose a recovery
  failure without weakening security assertions.
- **Proposed remediation:** Capture the exact `RecoverInternal` failure path
  under repeated full-suite execution, distinguish test isolation from a
  production recovery race, then fix the responsible layer with a red/green
  regression. Do not skip or relax either test.
- **Regression required:** Repeated full wallet `make check` runs and the
  affected recovery cases with the original assertions intact.
- **Final status:** OPEN pending root-cause investigation.

### FINDING-113: Crash replay rolls UTXO state forward without asset state

- **Severity:** HIGH. Asset ownership and issuance state can disagree with
  the recovered UTXO tip after an interrupted coins flush or reorganization.
  Whether a particular recovered state accepts an invalid block remains to
  be demonstrated.
- **Initial status:** OPEN on the uncommitted FINDING-107 context-propagation
  patch based on the existing `6d2a3f69f32bb295bf867e5cf7e872f6d5d15e69`
  tree. This finding was frozen before its remediation.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Restart and replay must produce the same
  asset state as uninterrupted block connection. The future PQ asset
  extension also depends on this invariant.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Chainstate-ahead recovery and its asset DB
  rebuild requirements. The missing asset-cache argument is already present
  in official Core 4.8.0 commit
  `22549129888d02e0e08fcdb9f96f3c699167e774`, so it is inherited.
- **Root cause:** `ReplayBlocks` creates an asset cache and passes it to
  old-branch disconnection, but omits it when calling `RollforwardBlock` for
  the new branch. `RollforwardBlock` therefore passes a null asset cache to
  `SpendCoin` and `AddCoins`; their asset updates are gated on a nonnull cache.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:5270-5287`,
  `RollforwardBlock`; `src/validation.cpp:5295-5354`, `ReplayBlocks`;
  `src/coins.cpp:103-105,264-265,383-385`, asset-cache gates.
- **Concrete scenario:** After an interrupted flush during a reorganization,
  replay undoes old-branch asset changes, advances the UTXO set to the new
  branch, and flushes an asset cache without applying new-branch issuance,
  transfers, or restrictions. A restarted node can then report or validate
  asset state differently from an uninterrupted peer.
- **Expected behavior:** Replay applies both UTXO and asset-cache changes for
  each rolled-forward block using that block's candidate-parent deployment
  context, then flushes coherent state.
- **Proposed remediation:** Pass the existing `assetsCache` to
  `RollforwardBlock` and verify an interrupted-flush or reorg replay against
  uninterrupted asset state. Do not redesign the replay mechanism.
- **Regression required:** A red/green replay test containing an asset
  issuance or transfer on the forward branch, including restart and asset DB
  comparison. Repeat with restricted state if practical.
- **Reproduction:** `feature_asset_dbcrash.py` generated an asset-active
  chain, flushed the parent, issued `REPLAYCRASHASSET` in block 433, and used
  `-dbbatchsize=1 -dbcrashratio=1` to exit after the partial coins batch.
  The debug log confirmed `Simulating a crash`, `Replaying blocks`, and
  rollforward of block 433. The block hash recovered, but `getassetdata`
  returned null instead of the previously observed issuance metadata.
- **Remediation commits:** `7f7b030209fdedbfe364ef54995c303f2486ff82`
  adds the red functional regression;
  `8e2a7037d57f78450e312429f0676711018eb0fd` passes the existing
  `assetsCache` into `RollforwardBlock`;
  `28b41f0824fe0e517bdaf0ed8ece4a328d487148` adds a second abrupt
  restart assertion after normal recovery startup.
- **Verification:** The focused functional test failed before the pointer
  fix and passed after it in two seconds on the same out-of-tree wallet
  build. The second abrupt restart, wallet `make check`, and no-wallet
  `make check` passed. A restricted-asset replay vector and exact-SHA CI
  remain due.
- **Final status:** FIXED for the demonstrated one-crash loss. The separate
  replay durability window in FINDING-114 remains open for release.

### FINDING-114: Coins flush clears recovery state before assets are durable

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. A power loss
  after the coins best-block write but before the asset DB dump can leave two
  honest nodes on the same block chain with different asset state and hence
  different asset-validation results. Invalid-block acceptance has not yet
  been demonstrated at the RPC or P2P boundary.
- **Initial status:** OPEN during adversarial review of the FINDING-113 fix.
  This finding was frozen before any remediation.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Restart, repeated interruption, and IBD
  recovery must produce coherent asset and UTXO state.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The official Core 4.8.0 code has the
  same ordinary flush ordering and replay ordering, with no durable marker
  spanning the coins and asset databases. This defect is inherited;
  FINDING-113 makes the replay variant visible in memory.
- **Root cause:** Ordinary `FlushStateToDisk` finalizes the coins best block
  and clears its head-block recovery marker before `DumpCacheToDatabase`
  writes the asset and restricted databases. `ReplayBlocks` likewise calls
  `cache.Flush()` before `assetsCache.Flush()`, which only merges changes into
  global memory. There is no durable asset-state proof linking the stores.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:5351-5355`,
  `ReplayBlocks`; `src/coins.cpp:482-486`, coins cache flush;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:2844-2856`, asset cache flush;
  `src/validation.cpp:3211-3223,5444`, ordinary and eventual asset DB flush;
  `src/txdb.cpp:211-218`, coins final batch.
- **Concrete scenario:** A block issues or reissues an asset. During an
  ordinary full flush, the coins final batch records the new best block and
  erases the head marker. A power loss before the asset DB dump leaves the
  asset DB at the prior state. Restart sees complete coins state and skips
  replay. The same gap appears if a first crash invokes `ReplayBlocks` and
  a second crash occurs before `RewindBlockIndex` dumps recovered assets.
- **Expected behavior:** A complete durable coins tip must never be visible
  on restart without matching durable asset state or a durable instruction
  to rebuild or replay both stores.
- **Proposed remediation:** Include an asset-commit-pending marker in the
  coins DB transition, retain it through the coins final batch, synchronize
  both asset and restricted databases after their dumps, and only then clear
  the pending marker. Startup with an uncertain complete coins tip must fail
  closed or rebuild after checking that required block data is available.
  Do not merely reverse the write order: an interruption during a partial
  asset DB dump may make replay non-idempotent.
- **Regression required:** Fault injection after the ordinary coins final
  batch and before asset DB persistence, plus a second interruption during
  recovery. Compare issuance, balances, and restrictions with an
  uninterrupted reference node and cover pruned-node failure handling.
- **Direct reproduction:** A regtest-only height hook stopped the node just
  after the ordinary coins flush for block 435 and before the asset dump.
  On the vulnerable startup path, the recovered block hash matched the
  mined block but `getassetdata(POSTCOINCRASHASSET)` returned null rather
  than the observed issuance metadata. The failing run was retained in the
  private audit evidence.
- **Remediation commit:** `ecd4ca0771a3e6d8a33bfe6b239ab9ed2e172e96`.
  The coins DB now records a pending asset-commit
  marker in both partial and final coins batches. A full flush dumps the
  asset cache, synchronizes the asset and restricted databases, then
  atomically clears pending and records a versioned validated tip with a
  synchronized coins DB batch. Startup rejects an incomplete or uncertified
  nonempty chainstate and rebuilds only when the block data is available.
  On a pruned or missing-data chainstate it stops without wiping databases.
  Legacy chainstates without a validated marker require one full rebuild.
- **Verification:** The same post-coins-flush functional test now
  passes, including issuance and reissue recovery. A new unit test checks
  pending, validated, stale, and unknown-version marker states. Wallet
  `make check`, no-wallet `make check`, and a five-test activation/crash
  functional batch pass. The additional vectors compare asset transfer
  balances and UTXO state with an uninterrupted node, reconstruct a
  restricted asset verifier and qualifier tag, and prove that truncated
  block data is detected before any automatic chainstate wipe.
  An independent source review found no additional ordering gap for the
  canonical asset or restricted state. A pruned-node fault test, exact-SHA
  CI, and final second audit remain due. Full-block preflight before a
  legacy rebuild adds substantial one-time I/O and must be assessed for
  deployment.
- **Test stabilization:** `87a7726b1` waits for the asynchronous
  restricted-state rebuild to reach the exact expected tip before the
  existing hash, metadata, and qualifier assertions. The test still
  fails if the rebuild does not complete within 60 seconds.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally for the reproduced inconsistent-state
  scenarios. Release qualification is still pending, including pruned-node
  handling and independent second review.

### FINDING-115: Reissue replay dereferences absent asset undo storage

- **Severity:** HIGH. A crash-interrupted coins flush whose forward block
  changes reissue metadata causes a startup segmentation fault, preventing
  normal recovery until code or chainstate is repaired.
- **Initial status:** OPEN after the FINDING-113 pointer fix, before this
  finding's remediation. The defect was frozen before a code correction.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Restart and IBD must reconstruct asset
  state safely and identically to uninterrupted block connection.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Asset-state crash replay. This precise
  null dereference was introduced by `8e2a7037d57f78450e312429f0676711018eb0fd`:
  official Core 4.8.0 left the asset cache null in rollforward, which
  avoided this dereference while losing asset state as FINDING-113 describes.
- **Root cause:** `RollforwardBlock` now passes a nonnull asset cache to
  `AddCoins` but still passes a null `undoAssetData`. On a reissue changing
  IPFS data, units, or verifier, `AddCoins` unconditionally writes the old
  metadata through that null pointer.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:5279-5287`,
  `RollforwardBlock`; `src/coins.cpp:124-188`, `AddCoins`.
- **Concrete scenario:** After a clean asset issuance, reissue it with a
  changed units field. Force a partial coins DB flush with
  `-dbbatchsize=1 -dbcrashratio=1`. The next startup enters replay and
  exits with signal 11 before RPC becomes available.
- **Expected behavior:** Replay may discard newly computed undo metadata
  because the block's original undo was already written, but it must pass
  valid scratch storage to `AddCoins` and must not crash.
- **Proposed remediation:** Supply a fresh local undo pair for each replayed
  transaction, or make the `AddCoins` undo output explicitly optional with
  guarded writes. Preserve the existing block undo semantics.
- **Regression required:** The real crash-replay functional test must
  include a reissue that changes units, IPFS data, or verifier, fail with
  the old code, and pass after the fix. Repeat under wallet and no-wallet
  unit gates.
- **Reproduction:** An extended `feature_asset_dbcrash.py` on the unmodified
  FINDING-113 fix failed at restart with `ravend exited with status -11 during
  initialization` after reissuing 5 units and setting the asset's units
  field to 1.
- **Remediation commits:** `06b93874d6f6b901d84dea85d58625598c5f4b18`
  adds the red reissue replay vector;
  `553c0b5a2c4209466bda75600d0de544675a319a` supplies transaction-local
  scratch undo storage to replay `AddCoins`.
- **Verification:** The same functional test failed before the fix with
  signal 11 and passed after the fix, including recovered amount 42 and
  units 1. Wallet and no-wallet `make check` both passed after the fix.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally for the reproduced crash; broader
  qualification and FINDING-114 release review remain pending.

### FINDING-116: Partial coins replay can double-count asset transfers

- **Severity:** HIGH. An interrupted
  coins batch can leave the asset balance/index cache different from a clean
  connection of the same block. A consensus-invalid block acceptance path
  has not yet been demonstrated.
- **Initial status:** OPEN during adversarial review after FINDING-113.
  This finding was frozen before remediation.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Restart and reorganization must yield the
  same asset ownership and amount state as uninterrupted validation.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The underlying partial-coins replay path
  is inherited. Official Core 4.8.0 passed no asset cache in rollforward and
  lost asset updates entirely; the FINDING-113 integration fix exposes a
  distinct non-idempotent transfer path.
- **Root cause:** `RollforwardBlock` ignores the return from `SpendCoin`.
  When the input coin was already erased by the interrupted DB batch,
  `SpendCoin` returns before `TrySpendCoin` can debit the old asset address.
  `AddCoins` then applies the transfer output asset credit unconditionally,
  even when replaying an already-applied output.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:5279-5288`,
  `RollforwardBlock`; `src/coins.cpp:361-389`, `SpendCoin`;
  `src/coins.cpp:257-280`, `AddCoins`.
- **Concrete scenario:** The partial coins batch has already erased the
  input UTXO of an asset transfer but has not completed the block. Replay
  sees the input missing, skips its asset debit, and credits the transfer
  output. The old and new address balances can both remain credited.
- **Expected behavior:** Crash recovery must reconstruct asset state from
  a known consistent parent or reject the ambiguous state; it cannot infer
  which asset-cache effects were applied from a partially written UTXO set.
- **Proposed remediation:** Fail closed on asset-active interrupted coins
  batches and rebuild coins, asset, and restricted state from indexed blocks
  after a pruned-data preflight. Do not make the asset-cache update depend
  only on whether an input UTXO happened to survive the partial batch.
- **Regression required:** A deterministic partial-batch transfer test that
  compares both address balances and chainstate against an uninterrupted
  reference, plus restart and pruned-node handling.
- **Regression:** `feature_asset_dbcrash_transfer.py` forces a partial coins
  batch crash while moving an asset from one address to another. After
  restart it compares both address balances, supply, metadata, and UTXO
  statistics to an uninterrupted reference node. The test passes with the
  safe-rebuild candidate; its expected startup log confirms that unsafe
  `ReplayBlocks` rollforward did not execute.
- **Remediation commit:** `ecd4ca0771a3e6d8a33bfe6b239ab9ed2e172e96`.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally for the reachable interrupted-startup path
  by the FINDING-114 rebuild guard. Final adversarial and cross-platform
  qualification remain due. The original `RollforwardBlock` remains
  non-idempotent, so the startup guard is security-critical.

### FINDING-117: Deep chain verification rejects a valid qualifier-tag block

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A valid chain containing a recently added qualifier
  tag fails `verifychain(4, 1)`. Nodes configured to perform level-4 startup
  verification can fail to start despite having a valid chainstate. A live
  consensus-split or invalid-block acceptance path has not been shown.
- **Initial status:** OPEN before remediation. The focused functional test
  failed on a normal uninterrupted chain, independently of FINDING-114's
  crash/recovery path.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Historical verification and reorganization
  must evaluate restricted-asset state at the block being checked.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** `CVerifyDB` copies the active asset cache
  then disconnects blocks in memory. The qualifier checker skips that local
  cache and consults the still-current global cache. Both behaviors are
  present in official Core 4.8.0, so this finding is inherited.
- **Root cause:** `VerifyQualifierChange` passes `fSkipTempCache=true` to
  `CheckForAddressQualifier`. This deliberately ignores modifications made
  within a connecting block, but during `CVerifyDB` reconnect it also
  ignores the historical state reconstructed in its private cache. The
  global cache still shows the tip's tag, making the original add appear
  to be a duplicate.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/validation.cpp:5193-5194,5238-5244,5258-5268`,
  `CVerifyDB::VerifyDB`; `src/assets/assets.cpp:4832-4900`,
  `CAssetsCache::CheckForAddressQualifier`; `src/assets/assets.cpp:5155-5167`,
  `VerifyQualifierChange`.
- **Concrete reproduction:** `feature_restricted_verifydb.py` activates
  restricted assets, issues a qualifier, mines one valid tag assignment,
  and calls `verifychain(4, 1)`. The call returns false. The debug log
  reports `bad-txns-null-data-add-qualifier-when-already-assigned` while
  reconnecting that same valid tip block. The tag is confirmed present by
  `checkaddresstag` before verification.
- **Expected behavior:** Level-4 verification uses the reconstructed
  pre-block restricted state while preserving the established rule that
  transactions inside a block do not make a qualifier change appear
  pre-existing to other transactions in that same block.
- **Proposed remediation:** Separate the historical pre-block cache from
  the current block's temporary changes in restricted-asset validation.
  Do not simply disable `fSkipTempCache` globally, because that could alter
  consensus checks for multiple restricted transactions in one block.
- **Regression required:** The red level-4 vector, a level-4 startup test,
  add/remove and freeze/unfreeze historical reconnection, and same-block
  ordering vectors.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` reconnects each historical block
  through a fresh child asset cache whose parent is the reconstructed
  pre-block state. The within-block skip now reads that parent.
- **Verification:** The previously red level-4 test, including startup
  verification, passes. The selected 12-test functional security gate
  and wallet-enabled and no-wallet `make check` pass locally.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; final independent review remains due.

### FINDING-118: Historical restricted verifier can remain stale during deep verification

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A valid restricted-asset transfer can make
  `verifychain(4, 7)` fail after the verifier string changes twice. A node
  configured for deep level-4 startup verification can reject a valid local
  chain. A live consensus-split path has not been demonstrated.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. The regression was found while adversarially
  extending the candidate remediation for FINDING-117. The candidate is
  uncommitted and does not resolve this case.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Historical validation must use the asset
  verifier in force at the block being reconnected. This also affects
  restricted-asset handling independent of PQ signing.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The cache insertion logic and
  `CVerifyDB` memory-only reconnect design exist in official 4.8.0. This is
  inherited, although the new pre-block cache path exposes it in a focused
  regression.
- **Root cause:** `CAssetCacheRestrictedVerifiers` set ordering keys only
  on asset name. `CAssetsCache::AddRestrictedVerifier` inserts a new verifier
  without removing the existing same-name entry. During a deep VerifyDB
  reconnect, the issuance verifier remains in the accumulated local cache;
  a later reissue's replacement insertion is a no-op.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assettypes.h:531-534`,
  `CAssetCacheRestrictedVerifiers::operator<`;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:2276-2285`,
  `CAssetsCache::AddRestrictedVerifier`;
  `src/validation.cpp:5193-5273`, `CVerifyDB::VerifyDB`.
- **Concrete reproduction:** `feature_restricted_verifydb.py` issues a
  restricted asset under a qualifier verifier, reissues it with verifier
  `true`, transfers to an untagged address, then reissues it back to the
  qualifier verifier. `verifychain(4, 3)` succeeds, but depth 7 fails while
  reconnecting the valid transfer at height 444 with
  `bad-txns-null-verifier-address-failed-verification`.
- **Expected behavior:** Each verifier update replaces the preceding local
  cache entry for that asset, including during deep historical reconnection.
- **Proposed remediation:** Replace an existing same-name add or remove entry
  during VerifyDB's memory-only replay, while preserving normal block
  connection semantics. Retain the deep level-4 regression.
- **Regression required:** Exercise the transfer on both sides of a
  verifier change at shallow and deep verification depths.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` replaces older verifier entries only
  in the historical aggregate, while each reconnected block uses normal
  first-insert semantics in its own child cache.
- **Verification:** The shallow/deep verifier test and the same-block
  first-wins unit test pass, as does the selected 12-test gate.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; final independent review remains due.

### FINDING-119: Later subqualifier tag corrupts historical root lookup

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. A valid restricted-asset transfer to an untagged
  address fails level-4 verification after that address receives a
  subqualifier. Startup with deep verification can reject a valid chain.
  No live invalid-block acceptance path has been demonstrated.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. The focused regression failed against the
  current FINDING-117 candidate before further remediation.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Historical asset verification must not
  retroactively apply a later tag to an earlier transfer. This is also a
  pre-existing restricted-asset behavior independent of PQ signing.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The root/subqualifier lookup and
  memory-only VerifyDB replay are inherited from official Core 4.8.0.
- **Root cause:** Undoing a `#ROOT/#SUB` tag records the subtag in the local
  `mapRootQualifierAddressesRemove`. `CheckForAddressQualifier` never
  consults that map before the live tip's root-add cache or database root
  prefix scan, so a historical `#ROOT` lookup can return true.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assets.cpp:2165-2182`,
  `CAssetsCache::RemoveQualifierAddress`;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:4834-4927`,
  `CAssetsCache::CheckForAddressQualifier`;
  `src/assets/restricteddb.cpp:148-169`,
  `CRestrictedDB::CheckForAddressRootQualifier`;
  `src/validation.cpp:5193-5273`, `CVerifyDB::VerifyDB`.
- **Concrete reproduction:** `feature_restricted_verifydb_root.py` issues
  a restricted asset with verifier `!#VERIFYROOT`, transfers it to an
  untagged address, then assigns `#VERIFYROOT/#SUB` in the next block.
  `checkaddresstag` confirms the root tag at the tip, while
  `verifychain(4, 2)` returns false reconnecting the earlier valid
  transfer.
- **Expected behavior:** Root qualification is computed from the
  reconstructed pre-block set of exact and subqualifier tags, excluding
  tags undone from later blocks.
- **Proposed remediation:** Make historical lookup account for local
  subqualifier removals before consulting live tip state, without changing
  ordinary consensus validation of same-block tag ordering.
- **Regression required:** The focused root/subtag vector, plus cases
  with multiple subtags so removing one does not hide another.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` checks exact-tag add/remove overlay
  for each root/subqualifier candidate instead of trusting a root map or
  live-tip database result alone.
- **Verification:** Historical root replay, multiple-subtag, persisted
  root, and two-node tests pass in the selected security gate.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; final independent review remains due.

### FINDING-120: Revoked subqualifier causes a live restricted-asset consensus split

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. Two honest nodes at the same best block can
  disagree about the validity of the next restricted-asset transfer. The
  dirty-cache node accepted and mined an unauthorized transfer; a restarted
  peer rejected the same block.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. The two-node red regression demonstrated the
  split before remediation. No release or push is permitted with this open.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** All nodes must validate restricted-asset
  ownership and transfers identically. The bug also affects Core 4.8.0
  independently of RIP-25 and predates the PQ asset extension.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Exact same bad insertion is present in
  official Core 4.8.0 commit `b60f50` at `src/assets/assets.cpp:2983-2986`.
  This is inherited, not introduced by the RIP-25 integration.
- **Root cause:** `CAssetsCache::Flush` merges local
  `mapRootQualifierAddressesRemove` entries into the global
  `mapRootQualifierAddressesAdd`. Removing the only `#ROOT/#SUB` tag leaves
  a false-positive root qualification in the dirty in-memory cache. A
  full asset database dump clears that cache and erases the exact subtag,
  so a restarted node correctly sees no root qualification.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assets.cpp:3025-3029`,
  `CAssetsCache::Flush`; `src/assets/assets.cpp:4834-4937`,
  `CAssetsCache::CheckForAddressQualifier`;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:1635-1647`,
  `CAssetTransfer::ContextualCheckAgainstVerifyString`;
  `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:736-743`, `Consensus::CheckTxAssets`;
  `src/validation.cpp:2756-2762`, `ConnectBlock`.
- **Concrete exploit:** `feature_restricted_root_split.py` creates two
  nodes with an identical tip. It removes the sole subtag from recipient
  R, then restarts one node. The uninterrupted node reports root
  qualification true; the restarted node reports false. The former
  accepts and mines a restricted transfer to R. The restarted node's
  `submitblock` returns `rejected` for that exact block. A separate
  single-node regression records `True` before restart and `False`
  after restart for the same chain tip.
- **Expected behavior:** Removing a subtag removes it from the global
  root-add cache, records its removal, and makes root qualification false
  unless another exact or subqualifier tag remains. Flush and restart
  must not change consensus validation.
- **Proposed remediation:** Correct the root-cache merge, then audit
  local/global add/remove precedence and database fallback. Preserve
  legitimate root qualification when one of multiple subtags remains.
- **Regression required:** The two-node accept/reject vector, same-tip
  pre/post-restart lookup, multiple-subtag boundary, and level-4
  historical verification after later tag changes.
- **Partial remediation commit:** `a4f2117e0` corrects both root-add and
  root-remove cache merge directions. It passes the in-memory split vector,
  but a stronger vector that persists the tag before revocation still
  fails: the database root-prefix lookup sees a now-revoked subtag.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` overlays the exact active state on
  each candidate subtag. The symmetric merge in `a4f2117e0` remains.
- **Verification:** The original dirty/restarted two-node split and the
  stronger persisted-tag variant pass. Removing one of two subtags keeps
  the root true; removing the last makes it false across dump/restart.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; final independent review remains due.

### FINDING-121: Serialized-key ordering splits persisted root qualification

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. Two honest nodes at the same block can disagree
  about a valid restricted-asset transfer when one has dumped its asset
  cache. The dirty node accepts the transfer; the flushed node rejects its
  block solely because an unrelated qualifier sorts before the subtag in
  LevelDB's serialized key order.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. A red two-node test demonstrated divergent
  qualification and block rejection during the second adversarial review.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Restricted-asset validation must be
  independent of cache flush timing and unrelated tags.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Official Core 4.8.0 commit `b60f50`
  has the same unsafe seek and early break in
  `src/assets/restricteddb.cpp:148-166`. This is inherited, not introduced
  by the RIP-25 integration. The initial replacement iterator inherited
  the same assumption and was rejected before commitment.
- **Root cause:** The database key contains a serialized `std::string`.
  Its CompactSize length precedes the string bytes, so a LevelDB seek to
  the textual root/prefix does not imply textual-prefix ordering. The
  iterator can encounter an unrelated same-length qualifier first and
  stop before a longer active subqualifier key.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/restricteddb.cpp:148-169`,
  `CRestrictedDB::CheckForAddressRootQualifier` (inherited);
  `src/assets/restricteddb.cpp:156-185`,
  `CRestrictedDB::AnyAddressSubQualifier`;
  `src/dbwrapper.h:139-153`, serialized iterator seek;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:4846-4925`, root qualification.
- **Concrete exploit:** `feature_restricted_root_prefix_split.py` tags
  a recipient with `#PREFIXROOT/#SUB` and an unrelated same-length
  `#ZZZZZZZZZZZ`. Two nodes share the same tip; one dumps the asset DB.
  The dirty node reports root qualification true and mines a restricted
  transfer. The flushed peer reports false and returns
  `duplicate-invalid` on submission of that block. The single-node
  cache test also changes from true to false immediately upon DB dump.
- **Expected behavior:** An active subqualifier remains visible regardless
  of other keys and whether the asset cache has been dumped.
- **Proposed remediation:** Seek separately for each valid serialized
  qualifier length, filter full qualifier names, overlay dirty exact-tag
  state, and stop as soon as a qualifying active subtag is found. Do not
  assume textual prefix order across different serialized lengths.
- **Regression required:** Two-node dirty/flushed block acceptance and
  equal-length unrelated-key boundary vectors.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` uses length-aware seeks in
  `AnyAddressSubQualifier` and exact-tag overlay for every candidate.
  `165f60448` isolates peer block submission in the regression test.
- **Verification:** The dirty/flushed two-node split, unrelated
  equal-length qualifier, cache dump, and restart cases pass.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; final independent review remains due.

### FINDING-122: Historical freeze overlays read the live tip before replay state

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. Level-4 verification rejects valid restricted
  transfers after a freeze/unfreeze cycle followed by a reorganization.
  A node configured for deep startup verification may reject a valid
  chain. No live accept/reject split from this replay-only path has been
  demonstrated.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. Three focused red regressions independently
  reproduce address-level freeze, global freeze, and verifier variants.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Historical asset checks must use the
  block's reconstructed pre-state while preserving the same-block skip
  policy. This is independent of PQ signature validity.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The checker precedence and VerifyDB
  memory-only reconnect design are inherited from official Core 4.8.0.
- **Root cause:** A historical cache can contain a local unfreeze add,
  while the live global cache contains a later freeze undo. The address
  and global restriction checkers inspect the live global remove set
  before the reconstructed local add, so the valid historical transfer
  appears frozen. The verifier-string checker has the same precedence
  pattern and selects a later live verifier over the historical one.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assets.cpp:4763-4831`,
  `CAssetsCache::GetAssetVerifierStringIfExists`;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:4940-5002`,
  `CAssetsCache::CheckForAddressRestriction`;
  `src/assets/assets.cpp:5003-5058`,
  `CAssetsCache::CheckForGlobalRestriction`;
  `src/validation.cpp:5193-5278`, `CVerifyDB::VerifyDB`.
- **Concrete reproduction:** `feature_restricted_verifydb_freeze.py` and
  `feature_restricted_verifydb_global.py` mine freeze, unfreeze, valid
  transfer, freeze, unfreeze, then invalidate the last unfreeze. The
  active tip is the second freeze; `verifychain(4, 4)` returns false
  while replaying the valid transfer. The address case logs
  `bad-txns-restricted-asset-transfer-from-frozen-address`.
  `feature_restricted_verifydb_verifier.py` changes the verifier from
  A to B, invalidates a later reissue, and observes
  `verifychain(4, 3)` reject a transfer valid under A.
- **Expected behavior:** During VerifyDB only, reconstructed local
  add/remove entries override the still-live global cache. Normal
  block connection and within-block consensus rules remain unchanged.
- **Proposed remediation:** Apply replay-scoped local-add precedence to
  verifier, address freeze, and global freeze checks, then run direct
  depth/reorg regressions for each.
- **Regression required:** Both frozen-scope red vectors and the verifier
  reissue/live-remove vector.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` scopes historical lookup precedence
  to the reconstructed parent while leaving normal block rules intact.
- **Verification:** Both freeze tests and the verifier/reorg test pass,
  including level-4 replay of valid historical transfers.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; final independent review remains due.

### FINDING-123: Mempool reissue map changes block consensus validation

- **Severity:** CRITICAL. Two honest nodes with the same chain but different
  pending reissues can accept and reject the same otherwise valid block.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. The focused test is red before remediation.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Block validity must depend on chain context,
  not a node's mempool. The issue is independent of PQ signatures.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** Inherited from official Core 4.8.0;
  `b60f50:src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp` contains the unconditional lookup.
- **Root cause:** `Consensus::CheckTxAssets` consults the global
  `mapReissuedAssets` mempool anti-chaining map even when its
  `fCheckMempool` argument is false in `ConnectBlock`.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/consensus/tx_verify.cpp:808-819`,
  `Consensus::CheckTxAssets`; `src/validation.cpp:2758`, `ConnectBlock`.
- **Introducing commit:** `f2a0d74c09` added the unconditional lookup;
  `080c1d5509` changed the rejection text.
- **Concrete reproduction:** After a reorg, an invalidated tip reissue
  returns to the mempool. A historical block containing a different valid
  reissue is rejected by `verifychain(4, 3)` with
  `bad-tx-reissue-chaining-not-allowed`. The test asserts the pending
  reissue is present in the mempool before the rejected replay. A live
  block carrying the historical reissue would likewise be rejected only
  by a node with the conflicting pending reissue.
- **Expected behavior:** The anti-chaining map may constrain mempool
  admission, but must never influence block acceptance or historical
  verification. Existing independent consensus checks remain intact.
- **Proposed remediation:** Apply the map conflict check only when
  `fCheckMempool` is true. Keep block-side reissue collection independent
  of the mempool map. Review block and mempool entry points separately.
- **Regression required:** The red reorg/mempool/VerifyDB test, a direct
  two-node block-acceptance split test, and existing asset reissue tests.
- **Remediation commits:** `b9b918b72` gates the map lookup on
  `fCheckMempool`; `927f8b9af` adds the live block test. The earlier
  verifier replay test acquired an explicit mempool precondition in
  `141527c49`.
- **Verification:** The live two-node test is red on the pre-fix binary
  (`submitblock` returns `bad-tx-reissue-chaining-not-allowed`) and green
  on the corrected binary. The verifier replay test is red before and
  green after the fix, with the conflicting reissue confirmed in mempool.
  `feature_restricted_assets.py` and `feature_raw_restricted_assets.py`
  also pass on the corrected binary.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; broader qualification and final-SHA
  review remain pending.

### FINDING-124: Historical replay changes same-block verifier ordering

- **Severity:** HIGH. Level-4 verification can reject a valid asset block,
  preventing startup when deep chain checking is enabled.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. A focused unit regression is red against the
  uncommitted VerifyDB remediation candidate.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The verifier state used to review a block
  must match the state used by normal block connection. This is separate
  from PQ cryptographic verification.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The first-insert verifier set behavior
  is inherited from official Core 4.8.0. The mismatch is introduced by
  the current historical-replay remediation candidate, not by 4.8.0.
- **Root cause:** `CAssetsCache::AddRestrictedVerifier` normally leaves
  the first same-name verifier in its per-block set. Replay mode erases
  that entry on every later add, so the second verifier wins even when
  both transactions are in one block.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/assets/assets.cpp:2276-2289`,
  `CAssetsCache::AddRestrictedVerifier`; `src/validation.cpp:5264-5274`,
  `CVerifyDB::VerifyDB`; `src/test/assets/asset_reissue_tests.cpp:205-227`.
- **Concrete scenario:** Two chained, consensus-valid restricted-asset
  reissues occupy one block; the second spends the first's returned owner
  token. They set different verifier strings. Normal connection retains
  the first value in its per-block cache, but replay substitutes the
  second. A later same-block reissue or transfer can therefore be
  accepted normally and rejected by VerifyDB. The unit regression
  observes replay `ACCREDITED` versus normal `KYC` and exits 201.
- **Expected behavior:** VerifyDB must use a fresh per-block asset cache
  with the same lookup and merge ordering as normal block connection,
  while accumulating historical state separately across blocks.
- **Proposed remediation:** Reconnect each block with a fresh child cache
  whose historical parent is the reconstructed cache; merge the child
  only after successful connection. Do not alter live consensus ordering.
- **Regression required:** The red same-block verifier unit test, the
  earlier historical verifier functional tests, and a multi-reissue
  full-block vector when practical.
- **Remediation commit:** `335095437` reconnects into a fresh per-block
  child and merges it into the historical parent after success. No full
  asset-cache copy occurs for each block.
- **Verification:** The unit test is red on the earlier candidate
  (replay `ACCREDITED`, normal `KYC`) and green after the fix; its
  next-block assertion verifies the later value replaces the earlier
  one. Wallet-enabled and no-wallet `make check` and the historical
  verifier tests pass. A full multi-reissue block vector remains desirable.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally for the demonstrated mismatch;
  full-block adversarial coverage remains pending.

### FINDING-125: Required CI gate omits new asset consensus regressions

- **Severity:** HIGH. A green security job could coexist with reopened
  asset consensus splits because the new tests were not in its required
  functional list.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. The required workflow list contains none of
  the F117-F124 root, replay, or mempool block-split tests.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The security qualification gate must run
  the actual consensus regression tests, not just inspect their files.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The omitted tests cover several
  inherited Core 4.8.0 asset-validation defects, plus integration fixes.
- **Root cause:** The tests were added after the explicit workflow list;
  most new scripts also lacked executable mode required by the runner.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `.github/workflows/build-raven.yml:80-88`, required functional step;
  `.github/workflows/rip25-v48-final-gate.yml:102-109`, matching step;
  `test/functional/test_runner.py:65-75`, registered extended tests;
  executable modes of new `feature_restricted_*` scripts.
- **Concrete reproduction:** Before gate integration, neither workflow
  names the new tests. After adding names but before correcting file
  modes, `test_runner.py --require-tests` executes the first test then
  raises `PermissionError` opening the next script. This fails closed,
  but prevents the required gate from qualifying the branch.
- **Expected behavior:** Both workflows explicitly name every security
  test, the runner knows each name, all scripts are executable, and
  `--require-tests` fails if any test is absent, skipped, or red.
- **Proposed remediation:** Register the new scripts, add them to both
  required workflow commands, set executable file modes, and run the
  exact selected list through the runner.
- **Regression required:** Execute the selected gate successfully and
  prove an unknown or failing named test produces a nonzero result.
- **Remediation commits:** `b3bcd5101` registers and requires the tests in
  both workflows and makes the new scripts executable; `7900d8ab4`
  corrects the previously included multiwallet script's executable mode.
- **Verification:** The selected 12-test `--require-tests --jobs=2` gate
  passes in 14 seconds locally. The complete 19-test workflow selection
  also passes. Before the second mode correction, that complete selection
  failed closed with `PermissionError` on `wallet_asset_multiwallet.py`.
  An unknown required test exits nonzero; the workflow YAML parses.
  GitHub exact-SHA execution remains pending.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; GitHub gate remains pending.

### FINDING-126: Inherited Python escape warnings fail the required asset test

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. The mandatory functional gate reports failure
  even though the asset RPC assertions pass, blocking qualification.
- **Initial status:** OPEN. The complete selected gate runs 11 of 12
  tests successfully, then flags `feature_restricted_assets.py` because
  it writes four `SyntaxWarning` messages to stderr.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Required security tests must execute and
  report their actual assertions, without false failures or skipped cases.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 behavior:** The four string literals are inherited
  unchanged from official Core 4.8.0 at `b60f50`.
- **Root cause:** Four Python string literals contain an unrecognized
  `\/` escape. Python 3.12 emits `SyntaxWarning`; the functional runner
  correctly treats unexpected stderr output as a failed test.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `test/functional/feature_restricted_assets.py:47,145,173-174`, invalid
  asset-name RPC vectors; `test/functional/test_runner.py`, stderr gate.
- **Expected behavior:** Keep exactly the same invalid asset-name bytes
  and the same RPC assertions, but express the literals without a warning.
- **Proposed remediation:** Prefix only these four literals with `r`.
  This preserves the backslash-plus-slash payload exactly.
- **Regression required:** Run the same selected `--require-tests` gate;
  confirm the asset test executes with no stderr and all 12 tests pass.
- **Remediation commit:** `c18d4b582` prefixes only the four literals
  with `r`, preserving their exact value and all RPC assertions.
- **Verification:** The same selected 12-test gate passes with no stderr
  from `feature_restricted_assets.py`.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; GitHub gate remains pending.

### FINDING-085: Chainstate recovery test observes an unfinished startup rebuild

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. The required functional security gate can reject a
  correct rebuild intermittently and block qualification. This is a test
  synchronization defect, not evidence that invalid chainstate is accepted.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `513a3bfc8db61c5539fb0c306508b8308397af85`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The mandatory Core 4.8.0 chainstate-ahead
  regression must test the completed rebuild without silently skipping it.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Automatic rebuild of coins, asset, and
  restricted-asset state when the persisted coins tip is ahead of the index.
  The defect is in the integration regression test, not inherited Core code.
- **Root cause:** `start_node()` waits for one successful `getblockcount()`
  RPC, while `ThreadImport` subsequently runs `ActivateBestChain` on a
  background thread. The test immediately compares the tip to the old index
  checkpoint even though the rebuild can still be connecting blocks.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `test/functional/feature_chainstate_ahead.py:63-75`, `run_test`;
  `test/functional/test_framework/test_node.py:94-107`,
  `wait_for_rpc_connection`; `src/init.cpp:1935,2027`, startup order.
- **Introduced by:** `22845c314c046a53800505a82960bce7d46b1cdf`.
- **Concrete scenario:** GitHub Final Gate run `36459106698` at
  `9d8b89a2d8a2fe1c3b26e6d1e861e3776b6c2c55` sees the recovery log but
  reads a noncheckpoint tip at line 71. A separate GitHub release security
  job on the same SHA passes, as do 12 repeated local three-test gate runs.
  The discrepancy is consistent with observing an intermediate startup tip.
- **Expected behavior:** Wait for the exact checkpoint tip within a bounded
  startup interval, then retain exact height and asset-state assertions. A
  persistently wrong tip or stale asset DB must still fail.
- **Proposed remediation:** Use the functional framework's bounded wait for
  the expected tip before the existing exact assertions. Do not relax the
  assertions or skip the test.
- **Regression required:** The original GitHub failure is red evidence;
  rerun the exact functional gate locally and in both GitHub workflows on
  the corrected SHA. Confirm the test still fails for a permanently wrong
  tip or asset state.
- **Remediation commit:** `cf4aea1d2`.
- **Verification:** The exact three-test functional gate passes locally.
  Twelve prior repeated runs passed, explaining why local-only validation
  missed the race. The bounded framework wait raises for a permanently false
  predicate; exact tip, height, and asset-state assertions remain unchanged.
  Both GitHub workflows still need a corrected-SHA rerun.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; GitHub exact-SHA rerun pending.

### FINDING-086: Legacy fontconfig source host rejects aarch64 release fetch

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. The aarch64 release artifact is unavailable, while
  the checksum-pinned dependency build fails closed and does not substitute
  a different source.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `cf4aea1d21bd85e78ccfe7e5ffecb31a714b174f`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Every official release target must build
  from an identical audited commit and checksum-verified dependency set.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The source URL and archive checksum are
  inherited unchanged from the official Core 4.8.0 dependency recipe. The
  observed HTTP rejection is an external availability problem in 2026, not
  evidence that the URL was broken at the 4.8.0 release date.
- **Root cause:** The upstream `www.freedesktop.org` archive endpoint returned
  HTTP 418 to GitHub's aarch64 runner; depends had no functioning fallback
  mirror and attempted an empty URL after that response.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `depends/packages/fontconfig.mk:3-5`,
  source acquisition for the 2.12.1 archive.
- **Concrete scenario:** GitHub Build Raven run `36459106688` at
  `9d8b89a2d8a2fe1c3b26e6d1e861e3776b6c2c55` built earlier dependencies,
  then failed fetching `fontconfig-2.12.1.tar.bz2` with HTTP 418 and no
  release artifact for aarch64. Other targets passed their build steps.
- **Expected behavior:** Obtain the exact 2.12.1 archive reliably and verify
  the existing `b449a3e10c47e1d1c7a6ec6e2016cca73d3bd68fbbd4f0ae5cc6b573f7d6c7f3`
  SHA256 before extraction. No version or contents change is authorized.
- **Proposed remediation:** Use an independently reachable archive mirror
  for the same checksum-pinned tarball. Do not remove or bypass checksum
  verification.
- **Regression required:** Fetch the candidate archive and compare SHA256
  with the official recipe; run the aarch64 GitHub artifact build on the
  corrected SHA and verify its produced archive and embedded source commit.
- **Remediation commit:** `580f408f5`.
- **Verification:** The MIT MacPorts mirror supplied the byte-identical
  `fontconfig-2.12.1.tar.bz2` archive with SHA256
  `b449a3e10c47e1d1c7a6ec6e2016cca73d3bd68fbbd4f0ae5cc6b573f7d6c7f3`.
  The depends recipe keeps the original version, filename, and checksum.
  GitHub aarch64 jobs passed and uploaded an unsigned artifact in Build Raven
  run `36518540863` at `5beba01c6b5b513b1a59a915e96cb6e65a5a3172`.
  The ultimate release SHA and artifact contents still require verification.
- **Final status:** FIXED; final-SHA artifact verification pending.

### FINDING-087: No-wallet release matrix cannot link the mandatory PQ derivation KAT

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. Three supported no-wallet cross-builds fail, blocking
  exact-SHA qualification. The defect does not bypass consensus validation.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `d6bfe2608defff885a67890927443ca74cfa60c5`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** The deterministic PQ derivation and full-chain
  KAT must execute in mandatory test binaries without silently being skipped.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. This was introduced by RIP-25 test
  integration, not inherited from official Core 4.8.0.
- **Root cause:** `test/pqkey_hardening_tests.cpp` is compiled in all builds
  and calls `pqderivation::GetKeypath` and `DeriveSeed`, but
  `wallet/pqderivation.cpp` is linked only through `libraven_wallet.a` when
  wallet support is enabled. The no-wallet test binary has unresolved calls.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `src/Makefile.test.include:73,119-134`,
  mandatory test source and conditional wallet link;
  `src/Makefile.am:324`, wallet-only derivation object;
  `src/test/pqkey_hardening_tests.cpp`, `rip25_production_full_chain_kat`.
- **Introducing provenance:** Post-checkpoint full-chain KAT/build wiring.
- **Concrete scenario:** Exact-SHA Final Gate run `36515177222` links
  `test/test_raven` in linux-disable-wallet, arm32v7-disable-wallet, and
  aarch64-disable-wallet jobs and fails with undefined references to both
  derivation functions. A wallet-enabled job links successfully.
- **Expected behavior:** The no-wallet test binary includes the small
  derivation implementation for its KAT while production no-wallet targets
  remain free of wallet linkage. Do not skip the test.
- **Proposed remediation:** Compile `wallet/pqderivation.cpp` into
  `test/test_raven` only when wallet support is disabled. Keep the existing
  wallet-library implementation for wallet-enabled builds.
- **Regression required:** Reproduce the no-wallet link failure from the
  GitHub log, then run a clean no-wallet configure/build and execute the
  full-chain KAT. Rerun all no-wallet GitHub matrix targets.
- **Remediation commit:** `08699b85f`.
- **Verification:** Clean out-of-tree no-wallet configure/build succeeded.
  The mandatory `rip25_production_full_chain_kat` executed 79 assertions
  successfully in the no-wallet binary; its complete `make check -j4`
  passed. All three no-wallet cross-target jobs passed in Final Gate run
  `36518540883` at `5beba01c6b5b513b1a59a915e96cb6e65a5a3172`.
- **Final status:** FIXED; final-SHA matrix confirmation pending.

### FINDING-088: Native release matrix test failure is hidden by the CI log

- **Severity:** MEDIUM pending root-cause classification. Exact-SHA
  qualification cannot pass or identify the failing case from the job log.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `d6bfe2608defff885a67890927443ca74cfa60c5`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Mandatory test execution must produce an
  auditable pass or actionable failure on the release matrix.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** Unknown until the failed test case is recovered.
- **Root cause:** The release matrix executes plain `make check`. Automake
  redirects test-case detail into `src/test-suite.log` on failure, and the
  workflow neither prints nor uploads that file.
- **Affected file/function/lines:**
  `.github/workflows/rip25-v48-final-gate.yml:222-226`, native test step.
- **Introducing provenance:** Integration final-gate workflow.
- **Concrete scenario:** Exact-SHA Final Gate run `36515177222` passes the
  required security-tests job, then its wallet-enabled Linux build compiles
  and reports `FAIL: test/test_raven` under `make check`. The live job log
  shows only the Automake summary and says to inspect `src/test-suite.log`,
  which is not exposed by the workflow. The failing test is not yet known.
- **Expected behavior:** Keep `make check` mandatory and failing, but display
  its test log on failure so the root cause can be identified and corrected.
- **Proposed remediation:** On `make check` failure, print bounded test logs
  and exit nonzero. Classify and separately remediate the underlying failed
  test after obtaining the evidence.
- **Regression required:** Verify the corrected workflow still fails for a
  failing `make check`, exposes test-case details, and rerun the exact-SHA
  native matrix. Do not mark the finding fixed solely from adding diagnostics.
- **Remediation commit:** `be3fd9e10` for log visibility only.
- **Verification:** The YAML parses, and a simulated failed `make check`
  exits nonzero while displaying the test log. Both Linux jobs passed in
  Final Gate run `36518540883`; the earlier underlying test failure was not
  reproduced, so its specific cause remains unclassified. The diagnostic
  correction remains in place for subsequent runs.
- **Final status:** MITIGATED; transient underlying failure under observation.

### FINDING-089: macOS packaging loses the pinned Python path inside make

- **Severity:** MEDIUM. The macOS release artifact cannot be produced, but
  consensus code and the dependency checksums are unchanged.
- **Frozen initial status:** OPEN at `5beba01c6b5b513b1a59a915e96cb6e65a5a3172`.
- **Affected RIP-25 invariant:** Every supported release artifact must be
  built from the exact audited source with pinned dependency provenance.
- **Affected Core 4.8.0 fix:** None. The Makefile environment precedence is
  inherited from Core 4.8.0; the incorrect package-script assumption was
  introduced in this integration's CI workflow.
- **Root cause:** The package script exports the correct pinned two-directory
  `PYTHONPATH` and directly imports `ds_store`, `mac_alias`, and `biplist`
  successfully. The generated Makefile also assigns `PYTHONPATH` from
  configure and exports it to recipe subprocesses, overriding the shell
  environment when `make deploydir` runs. Its value omits the pinned
  `python3/dist-packages` directory containing `ds_store`.
- **Affected file/function/lines:** `.github/scripts/06-package.sh:101-123`,
  macOS package branch; `Makefile.am:14`, exported configure variable;
  generated `Makefile.in`, `PYTHONPATH = @PYTHONPATH@`.
- **Introducing provenance:** Integration macOS package-script path setup.
- **Concrete scenario:** Build Raven run `36515178744` at
  `d6bfe2608defff885a67890927443ca74cfa60c5` passes the pinned-module
  preflight and compiles the app, then `make deploydir` executes
  `custom_dsstore.py` with the configured path and raises
  `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'ds_store'`.
- **Expected behavior:** Both `make deploydir` and `make deploy` propagate
  the exact preflight-checked pinned `PYTHONPATH` into recipe subprocesses.
- **Proposed remediation:** Pass `PYTHONPATH` as a command-line make variable
  for these package targets; leave dependency acquisition and source intact.
- **Regression required:** Demonstrate an exported shell `PYTHONPATH` loses
  to the configured Makefile assignment, demonstrate command-line override
  wins, and rerun the complete macOS artifact job on the corrected SHA.
- **Remediation commit:** `87b38a86e`.
- **Verification:** Direct make evaluation proves an exported environment
  `PYTHONPATH` loses to the configured Makefile value, while a command-line
  make assignment wins. The macOS package script now uses that assignment
  for both affected targets. `bash -n` and diff whitespace checks pass;
  the exact-SHA macOS artifact rerun is pending.
- **Final status:** FIXED locally; GitHub macOS artifact pending.
